## §7.18 · Audit Closure and the Real WA Reference Data (18 September 2026)

**Branch:** `ward/audit-and-real-data-20260918`, off `cfff5dd54e`.
**Inputs:** the consolidated audit (`⭐⭐⭐⭐WARD-FLOW-CONSOLIDATED-AUDIT-2026-09-17`), the research
handover ZIP, and the in-repo pack at `docs/ward-flow/reference-data/` — which was already at Phase 5
with **Phase 6 recorded as "NOT STARTED — Joshua-gated"**. This session is that Phase 6, scoped by the
owner on 18 September, plus the audit's real defects.

### 1. What the audit got wrong, and this is the more useful half of the record

Every claim was re-verified against the working tree before being acted on. The audit was written
against `f10ca39fbd`; the tree had moved five commits past it.

| Audit finding                                                        | Verdict after measurement                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **P1 ED board horizontal overflow** (desktop 2013/2513, tablet 1769) | 🔴 **FALSE.** At 1440×900 `document.body.scrollWidth` equals the viewport, and at 820×1180 it is 805 against a 805px viewport with **zero elements outside a scrollable ancestor**. `globals.css:915,948` set `overflow-x: clip` on `html` and `body`, so `documentElement.scrollWidth` reports an unclipped descendant box nothing can scroll to — 2040 desktop, 1294 tablet. **Measuring `documentElement` finds a bug that is not there and "fixing" it breaks a working scroller.** The board's sideways scroll is what the owner asked for (E.51). Commit `6fd766d37c` had already closed the real issue. |
| **P1 WLQ-4: guard EN_ROUTE/ARRIVED while an examination is revoked** | 🔴 **WOULD HAVE REVERSED AN OWNER RULING.** `ward-flow-reducer.ts:2632-2700` records WLQ-4 (owner, 2026-09-15): once transport is committed, do neither automatically — the bed stays held and the vehicle is not stopped. The same block says adding a transport guard is "for the owner rather than decided here". Not built.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| **P1 tablet: intake Send disabled without a reason**                 | 🔴 **FALSE.** `referral-intake.tsx:2565-2582` already renders "Not answered: …" or "Too long to send: …" with `role="alert"` and `aria-describedby`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| **P1 tablet: community card / gateway link not clickable**           | 🔴 **FALSE.** 86 working team links at 820px, each resolving to a real `/community/[teamId]`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| **P1 tablet: raise-referral link not visible**                       | ⚠️ **Imprecise.** It renders and is reachable by scrolling (6092px down a 7370px page). The real defect underneath is that it is **99×28px**, part of the audit's own sub-44px tap-target class (P2), deferred here.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| **P0 statutory chrome**                                              | ✅ **REAL**, but its prescribed fix was wrong — see §2.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| **P1 CONFIRM_CAPACITY unbounded**                                    | ✅ **REAL**, and narrower than stated — see §3.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |

### 2. Legal wording — the claim stops, the facts stay

Owner decision, 18 September: _"stop the claim, keep the facts."_ 25 rendered strings across 13 files.

**Banned** — the app presenting itself as an official register or enforcement tool, or calculating a
legal deadline it has no authority to calculate: "Statutory Forms Registry", "Statutory Breaches",
"Issue MHA 2014 Statutory Form", "Form 1A Strict Enforcement", "Legal deadline passed".

**Kept, because the audit's blanket strip would have broken working clinical logic:** the official
form titles (`ward-legal-forms.ts` records the owner approving them); the `authorisation` eligibility
gate and every sentence explaining why an involuntary patient cannot go to a particular ward
(`ward-eligibility.ts:113-127`); and the Voluntary/Involuntary transport vocabulary.

**No behaviour changed.** `legal-forms-derivations.ts` reads `legalForm.dueAt` — an expiry a clinician
typed — and formats the time remaining. Only the word "deadline" ever claimed the app worked it out.

`npm run check:ward-legal-language` guards it. Comments are blanked before matching, so writing the
rule down does not fail the rule.

### 3. The reducer trust boundary

`CONFIRM_CAPACITY` now refuses a count above `unit.beds`, or a negative or fractional one, naming
both figures. **The bound is `unit.beds`, never `empty.value`** — a ward restating what it can
allocate is allowed to disagree with a stale feed, and the "arrival capacity floor" test depends on
exactly that. It **refuses rather than clamps**: clamping 30 to 20 records a number the ward never
said. Severity was lower than the audit implied — state could not be corrupted, the exposure was a
displayed figure.

Step-back already said "It does not release a bed" on every movement, which is what made it easy to
read past. It now also names the held bed and points at "Release the pulled bed", **only when one is
actually held**.

### 4. Phase 6 — the real WA data, names only

|                           |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| ------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| **Registry**              | 98 entities generated into `reference/ward-reference-registry.ts`: 28 facilities, 12 EDs, 14 named wards, 12 named units, 32 community services.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| **The boundary**          | 🔴 **`ReferenceEntity` has no numeric field of any kind.** Not `staffed_beds`, not a capacity assertion. There is nothing for an unratified figure to leak through — no import to police, no rule to remember. The pack's INGEST_MAP recommends a CI rule forbidding `staffed_beds`; a type with no number in it does not depend on anyone reading one. Graylands is published as both 109 and 122 beds with "prefer neither" standing, which is why this matters.                   |
| **Ward names**            | 9 of 23 demo wards now carry real names — Ward 2K, Mental Health Unit, Moodjar, Maali, Wardong, Great Southern APU, Bunbury APU, Mabu Liyan, EMyU. **No id changed** (ids are routes and selectors). **The other 14 keep invented names**: the pack records no adult ward at Rockingham, none at Kununurra or SJG Subiaco. An honest gap beats a guessed name. Two mappings are ambiguous and recorded as such: Armadale has two adult wards in the pack, Fremantle three open ones. |
| **Emergency departments** | King Edward Memorial and Perth Children's added, both `units: []` — neither has an adult mental-health ward, which is the point rather than a gap. Hollywood and SJG Murdoch are private, out of scope.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| **Community teams**       | The search hub listed **ten "(placeholder)" region names** while the rest of the app used **64 real catchment-derived teams**. Retired, per owner ruling Q-5. This also closed a documented broken link: hub rows pointed at the bare index because a region slug resolved to no team.                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| **Distances**             | 468 measured metropolitan road pairs shown on the coordinator's shortlist. 🔴 **`TRAVEL_BANDS_ARE_INVENTED` stays `true`** — those bands map WA _home regions_ and cover Kimberley, Pilbara, Mid West and Great Southern; the pack has **no regional pair at all**. Filling one from the other would answer a different question with a real-looking number. The generator throws if a regional pair ever appears.                                                                   |

**Approval flags are untouched.** `operational_use_approved` and `automatic_routing_approved` remain
`false` on every row. No bed count, no capacity assertion and no catchment entered the app. Phase 7
is not started. The 46 research questions and 18 pack P0 questions are unaffected.

🔴 **Real Noongar ward names are now in the app** — Moodjar, Karri, Yorgum, Waalitj, Maali, Wardong,
Djiti djiti, Boodalung, Dryandra, Kogolup, Dabakarn, Mabu Liyan. The owner deferred the Aboriginal
cultural-safety review on 17 September and asked not to be asked again. **It is recorded here, not
re-raised, and it remains a hard gate before any real-patient use.**

### 5. Two reds this branch inherited rather than caused

- 🔴 **`tests/ward-event-reachability.test.ts` was already failing at `6fd766d37c`.** `REOPEN_INBOX_ITEM`
  has never been dispatched by any screen — `git grep` at both `6fd766d37c` and `cfff5dd54e` finds it
  only in the reducer, the event union and the persistence classifier. So a coordinator can mark an
  inbox item done and cannot put it back. Recorded in `KNOWN_UNREACHABLE` with its reason rather than
  fixed: closing it needs a control and a decision about who may reopen. **The ward suite was not
  green at the tip and nobody knew.**
- **`RECORD_TRANSPORT_NEED` was built, tested, correct and unreachable.** Now has a "No transport
  needed" button on the ED board (owner ruling, 17 Sept, round 2 item 10). `RECORD_NO_REFERRAL`
  deliberately stays in the allowlist — no ruling covers it.

### 6. Three mistakes worth keeping

1. **A `·` Map separator became 468 raw NUL bytes** in a generated source file. Tests, tsc and
   Prettier all passed; `check-source-control-chars.mjs` caught it — the gate written after the same
   thing happened on 2026-09-10. Separator is now `|`.
2. **A `data-testid` beginning `ward-shortlist-candidate-`** made new spans count as candidate rows,
   so a test clicking one selected nothing. A testid prefix is a namespace other files select on.
3. **The distance caveat went into a `title` tooltip** — the exact defect this panel had already
   removed once, its own test pinning the absence because a tooltip is invisible on touch and by
   keyboard. Now rendered as text.

### 7. Left undone, deliberately

- **`ward-teams.ts` is not deleted.** The ward board still derives a team from a patient's home
  region for its "where these beds free up to" panel. Replacing that means deciding what a
  region-level aggregate shows when no team may be derived from home area (owner ruling, 17 Sept,
  round 2 item 20) — a design question, handed back.
- **Community-team contact detail not wired.** The plan was to enrich each team with the pack's
  published phone, hours and referral email, matched **by exact name only** — the pack forbids
  matching on suburb, postcode or proximity by name. The app's 64 names are short catchment cells
  ("Albany", "Alma Street (Fremantle)"); the pack's 32 are full official titles ("Butler Community
  Mental Health Service"). Exact matching yields almost nothing. Linking them needs a hand-authored
  mapping, one judgement per team — the owner's call.
- **The seed `it.fails`** stays red by design. Measured: **all eight** movements at `pulled` lack an
  `admissionId`, not a few — the field is never set on a movement literal anywhere, so the seed does
  not model a held bed at rest and only `PULL_PATIENT` ever sets it. Closing it means authoring eight
  admissions agreeing exactly with each unit's `sexMix`: a modelling decision, not a fixture top-up.
- **The P2/P3 hygiene list**: sub-44px tap targets (measured above at 28px on two controls), the "No
  reconciliation…" empty copy, generic ED page titles, duplicate slug variants, the two candidate
  APIs.
- **66 `file://` links** in two handover documents point into one machine's Antigravity worktree and
  resolve for nobody. Reported by `check:ward-doc-links`, not failed: repointing them needs content
  only their author ever had. All 16 genuinely broken **relative** links are fixed.

### 8. New gates

`check:ward-legal-language` · `check:ward-reference` (both generated files, drift-checked) ·
`check:ward-doc-links` · `ward:reference:build`.

### 9. The fold, and what it proved (18 September 2026, 04:30)

`ward/audit-and-real-data-20260918` (20 commits) and the peer session's
`claude/ward-remeasure-distances-20260918` (4 commits) are both home on the ward line
at `fa67a2b831`. Typecheck exit 0 on the merged tree.

⚠️ **`screen-verification.json` arrived mid-write and malformed** — the named risk of snapshotting
a live tree, now concrete. Restored from the committed copy and re-annotated.

`D:/Worktrees/Database/ward-audit-realdata-20260918`. Benign on inspection (its own
patient-now adapter types; typecheck clean, 51 tests green afterwards) — but **an isolated
worktree is not isolated from an unattended tool**, and no session on this machine should
assume otherwise.

**Form 5A, three times, from one document.** "Form 5A (Involuntary Inpatient Treatment Order)"

⚠️ **AND THAT SAME RUBRIC AWARDS 25 POINTS** for forms "modeled with countdown timers and expiry
states" — the exact behaviour the legal lane removed. **A scoring document still in the handover
pack pays the next agent to re-introduce what this session swept out.** For the owner.

**Still open, named rather than absorbed:** the union suite result; `ward-flow-chat-control`, whose
worker crashes at 25/38/42 of 44 as machine load varies and which never fails an assertion;
the mockups' own form mispairings, which sit outside every code guard.

The path recorded here was `add-a-patient-third-edition.html:761`, and that string lives in

And the fresh screen looks owed on eleven annotated records.

#### §7.18.10 The union suite, and what 24 reds turned out to be

**Measured on the merged tree at `16de6bb4f4`, 2026-09-18.** `check:ward-expected-reds` reported
**24 ward test files failing and not in the manifest.** The number on its own is useless, so every
one of the 24 was re-run in isolation and each failure read for the file it names.

**One of the 24 does not reproduce.** `ward-legal-figure-guard.test.ts` passes on its own, twice,
in two different groupings. ⚠️ **This machine's vitest worker forks crash under load** — the same
run reported `ward-flow-chat-control` at 25, 38 and 42 of 44 passing on three attempts without ever
failing an assertion. **So a whole-suite count here is an UPPER BOUND, not a measurement**, and a
red that nobody reproduces in isolation is not yet a defect.

**One of the 24 was mine, and it was the reference layer colliding with the one-home rule.**
`ward-flow-data-boundary.test.ts` holds the owner's instruction that every changeable fact has one
editable home, and `reference/ward-reference-registry.ts` states "Royal Perth Hospital" and
"Ward 2K" — which reads exactly like a second home. 🔴 **The guard was right to fire and the answer
was not to widen it quietly.** The registry is generated from
`docs/ward-flow/reference-data/entities.json` and `check:ward-reference` reddens on any hand-edit,
so it cannot silently disagree with anything; it is recorded as the tenth `DATA_LAYER` entry with
that reasoning written beside the other nine, and the file's own canary bumped from 9 to 10 so the
entry cannot be added without somebody noticing. The SECOND offender in the same test —
`search/record-preview.tsx` typing `"Bentley Health Service"` into a demo fixture — was a genuine
second home and now reads the name through `siteByCode("BTY")`.

**The other 22 trace to the concurrent rebuild, not to this work.** Attributed by reading the file
each failure names, then `git blame` on the line:

- **A ward screen now lists every other ward.** `ward/ward-screen.tsx:1231` renders
  `allUnits().map(...)` into a "Change Ward" `<select>`, each option carrying `{u.name} ({u.beds}
beds)`. It arrived in `cfff5dd54e`, the protective snapshot of the other session's in-flight work.
  It reddens **two** guards at once: `ward-flow-single-source` (an unallowlisted read of the units
  fixture) and `ward-override-register-render`, whose FD-23/OD-3 case asserts that **no** other
  ward's name may appear anywhere in `document.body` of a ward's own screen. 🔴 **For the owner, and
  deliberately not decided here: the 2026-08-31 ruling is about what a ward may learn of a PATIENT's
  other referrals, and a static roster of ward names is not that — so the likely answer is that the
  assertion at `:302` is now wider than the ruling it defends. But it is a clinical privacy ruling,
  the bed counts beside each name are a separate visibility question nobody has asked, and
  ⚠️ narrowing a privacy assertion to make a new feature pass is the exact move that must never be
  made by whoever wants the feature.** Recorded, not resolved.
- **Design-system reds follow the rebuilt stylesheets.** `governance-third-edition.module.css` (14
  mentions), `legal-forms/legal-forms.module.css` (13) and `statistics-landing-third-edition.module.css`
  (12) account for most of the raw-hex, token-resolution, breakpoint, table-min-width and
  duplicate-rule failures across seven files.
- **Route and control counts moved with the new screens.** `ward-route-component-binding` 34 vs 35,
  `ward-primitives-shared` 39 vs 38, `ward-no-dead-ends` 13 unrecorded dead controls (`Inspect Form`
  anchors on the officer screen), `ward-instant-display` on `answer.at`,
  `ward-chrome-owner` on `.answerHeader` — all from `ward-answer-view.tsx` and the sovereign
  showcase.
- **`ward-nav`'s community-route coverage moved from "0 of 64" to "1 of 64"** because
  `ward-flow-sign-in-data.ts:312` now carries a concrete `"/mockups/ward-flow/community/midland"`.
  Added in `48ffbfc524`, the second protective snapshot. ⚠️ **I looked for this in the hub, because
  §7.18's task D4 had just pointed the hub at the 64 real teams and the number was the number D4
  produced.** The plausible author is not the author; `git blame` is.

**What this says about the fold, stated exactly.** The protected snapshot was already failing ~21
ward test files before any of this session's work. The merged tree reports 24, of which 1 does not
reproduce and 1 was mine and is closed. ⚠️ **These two figures were counted by different methods on
different trees and must not be subtracted from one another** — see
`memory/name-the-tree-before-disputing-the-method.md`. What IS safe to say: every failure this
session could attribute belongs either to the reference layer's arrival (closed) or to the
concurrent rebuild (open, and open before the merge).

**Confirmed after the fix, same tree, `1b52b95208`: 24 → 22.** The two that cleared are exactly the
two accounted for above — `ward-flow-data-boundary` (mine, closed) and `ward-legal-figure-guard`
(the non-reproducing worker crash, which did not recur on this run and so was never a defect).
**The remaining 22 are the list below, unchanged in membership, and every one of them was failing
before this session's work was merged.** They are not entered in `ward-expected-reds.json`: an
entry there states a red is deliberate and says whose question holds it open, and none of these is
deliberate — they are the concurrent rebuild's open work, and burying them in a manifest would
convert somebody else's unfinished screen into a permitted red.

#### §7.18.11 Two reds that are the same question, and it is the owner's

Claimed by Ward Lead out of the 22 because both guards refuse, in their own words, to be resolved
by whoever wants the feature. **Neither is fixed here, and that is the finding.**

**`ward-patient-link-default-deny`** names three files reading `.patientId` outside the D-14
allowlist: `alerts/alerts-screen.tsx:87-88`, `patients/patient-now-adapter.ts:40,59` and
`search/patient-search.tsx:1037,1196`. All six reads blame to `cfff5dd54e`, the protective snapshot
of the other session's in-flight work — **none is this session's.** Most join a referral to a
patient within one ward's own view, which is probably fine; `patient-search.tsx:1196` runs
`referrals?.find((r) => r.patientId === patient.id)`, which is a lookup FROM a patient TO their
referrals, and that is the shape D-14 exists to stop. 🔴 **The guard's failure message says
verbatim: "Hand this back to Ward Lead rather than deciding it is fine and widening the allowlist
yourself." Ward Lead's answer is that this is not a Ward Lead decision either** — it is the same
clinical-privacy ruling as the item below, on a feature neither of us authored.

**`ward-override-register-render` + `ward-flow-single-source`** — the "Change Ward" select at
`ward/ward-screen.tsx:1231`, recorded in §7.18.10.

⚠️ **These are ONE question and must be put to the owner once.** Both ask what a ward may learn
about patients and wards that are not its own, both arrived in the same snapshot, and splitting
them into two asks is how one ruling comes back in two halves that do not agree — see
`memory/the-question-belongs-to-the-answer.md`. Recorded together, asked together, neither touched.

✅ **What was deliberately NOT done:** widening either allowlist. A privacy assertion narrowed so a
new feature passes has been converted from a control into a decoration, and the party doing the
narrowing is always the party who wants the feature. Both guards stay red until he answers.

#### §7.18.12 The third protective snapshot, and an observation I let expire

**Committed 2026-09-18 17:05: 33 files that had sat uncommitted for nearly seven hours.**

🔴 **I am recording why this was late, because the cause was mine.** At 10:2x I measured the other
tool actively writing — 31 files mid-edit — and told a peer session so, advising against starting
work in those files. It was true when written. A peer re-measured and sent it back: newest mtime
among the 35 was **10:10:27**, clock **17:04:43**. ⚠️ **"Something is writing right now" is the most
perishable observation there is, and I published it as a standing condition and then obeyed my own
sentence for seven hours.** The cost was not a wrong fact. It was that thirty-five files — by this
repository's own rule the ONLY work this machine can lose — stayed unowned, while the one session
that could have committed them was declining to be a second committer in my folder. **Nobody chose
that; it happened by nobody re-measuring.**

✅ **And the refusal did not flip automatically when its reason expired.** The reason not to snapshot
was that a mid-write file had already produced a malformed `screen-verification.json` hours earlier.
Quiet is not intact, so the snapshot was validated BEFORE it was taken, not after:
`screen-verification.json` parses · `check:ward-source-control-chars` clean over the tree — the gate
that caught 468 NUL bytes in a generated file this week · `tsc -p tsconfig.typecheck.json --noEmit`
exit 0. Then 33 paths staged by name, the two mockup server logs deliberately left untracked, then
`backup-work.sh`: 7,643 files, 4.0G, at `C:/Users/joshs/Backups/claude-work/2026-09-18T090608Z`.

ward-statistics" while `statistics-v4-primitives-tokens` is red on the same tree.

**What the snapshot changes and what it does not.** It makes the 22 reds attributable to a tree
somebody owns, which is the difference between a number that can be acted on and a number measured
against unowned edits. **It makes nothing less red, and it endorses nothing** — no ward test was run
over this state by this session before committing it, and the commit message says so.

#### §7.18.13 Half the owner question answered itself, and the count on the owned tree

rather than simply shrinking — `ward-override-register-render` and `ward-pull-vocabulary` left,

the identifier match while doing the identical thing, which is disarming the guard rather than
satisfying it.

#### §7.18.14 An unidentified writer, a public tunnel origin, and two attributions we both got wrong

**18:05–18:24, 2026-09-18. Something is writing into `D:/Worktrees/Database/ward-lead` and neither
live Claude session is it.** Recorded here because the file it changed is invisible to every gate in
this repository, so the ledger is the only place the fact can survive.

🔴 **`next.config.ts` — `allowedDevOrigins: ["127.0.0.1"]` became
`["127.0.0.1", "*.trycloudflare.com"]`.** Uncommitted, written 18:05:46, author unknown. That is a
wildcard over Cloudflare's free public tunnel service: **any** `*.trycloudflare.com` host — anyone's
tunnel, not only one we opened — becomes an origin this project's dev server will accept
cross-origin requests from. Dev-only, and the ward line is never pushed, so nothing reaches
production from here. **But the dev server is the one that talks to the live Supabase project when
env is present**, and `allowedDevOrigins` exists precisely to stop a foreign origin reaching a dev
server's internals.

⚠️ **Nothing in this repository can see it.** `grep -rn "allowedDevOrigins" tests/ scripts/` returns
nothing and `trycloudflare` appears in exactly one file — the line itself. No test, no static gate,
no lint rule. It is recorded verbatim above so that the fact outlives the file whichever way the
owner rules. **For the owner: keep the tunnel origin, or restore `["127.0.0.1"]`?**

**AND THE PART WORTH MORE THAN THE FINDING: we both misattributed, the same way, two hours apart.**

I told the peer session, with a measurement attached, that its agents 2 and 3 were writing here. The
measurement was real — 11 files, mtimes seconds old, matching two agents' declared scope exactly.
🔴 **The inference was not.** The peer killed all three agents at 18:07 and the writes continued for
another seventeen minutes; `ListAgents` showed one completed subagent, whose work was correctly in
its own worktree. The newest write — `ward/ward.module.css` splitting `:hover` from
`:focus-visible` and adding `outline: 2px solid var(--accent)` — is a focus-visible accessibility
fix in nobody's brief, and `board.module.css` and `officer.module.css` were never in the scope I
assigned them to.

⚠️ **Scope-matching is pattern-matching, and I had caught myself doing exactly this on `ward-nav`
four hours earlier**, where 64 was "obviously" the hub's number and `git blame` put it in
`ward-flow-sign-in-data.ts`. I published the corrected method to the peer and then broke it myself
on the next question. **A plausible author is not an author. The only instruments that answer
authorship are `git blame`, the reflog, and a process listing — and when the file is uncommitted,
none of them can, which is the honest answer rather than a reason to guess.**

✅ **What that error nearly cost.** The peer had asked the owner for permission to discard eight of
these edits as "only my own agents' unverified work" — a description built on my attribution. It
withdrew the request before he answered. Had he agreed, another tool's work would have been
destroyed on a confident misreading, and the peer's own captured patch would have been the only
copy. **It was withdrawn because a session re-measured its own claim rather than defending it.**

**State at 18:24: 22 files, still being written, NOT snapshotted.** §7.18.12's rule holds — quiet
first, validate, then commit. Backups taken meanwhile at `2026-09-18T100630Z` and
`2026-09-18T101658Z` cover work whose author neither session can name.

`~/.claude/worktree-ownership.md`; needs the owner's hand or a permission rule.

#### §7.18.16 Q-12 narrowed three times, and what was actually lost on Delays

**Three readings of the same change, each narrower than the last. The final one is the only one that

1. **Mine (§7.18.15): "a ruling may have been written out of its own guard."** Evidence: `grep -c

   labelling its own grep output "(none in tests)" when the matches it had truncated away were an

the sentence above:** the headline disclosure survives. `delays-screen.tsx:437-441` still renders a

Nothing here is a clinical record."_ **What was lost is the finer-grained provenance** — which

**For the owner, narrowly:** was the Delays provenance panel meant to go with the third-edition

## §7.19 · Owner Rulings, Clinical Queue Prioritisation, and Operational Task Re-baseline (18 September 2026)

**Recorded 18 September 2026.**  
**Reference Document**: [`ward-flow/archive/dated-notes/owner-answers-2026-09-18.md`](ward-flow/archive/dated-notes/owner-answers-2026-09-18.md) (authoritative rulings and clinical rationales recorded verbatim).  
**Context**: On 18 September 2026, Josh provided 18 definitive rulings and instructions resolving outstanding architectural, clinical workflow, and interface decisions across the Ward Flow estate.

### 1. The 18 Rulings and Operational Decisions

1. **Ruling 1 (`WF-16`) — Non-Transport Handover Progression [APPROVED]**:
   - _Ruling_: **Yes** — non-transport handover progression.
   - _Clinical Rationale_: Transfers not requiring transport vehicles (e.g. family transport, walking escorts between adjacent facilities, internal campus movements) must not require an artificial transport booking. `HANDOVER_READY` readiness checks are satisfied when `transport.needed === false`, progressing directly to ward arrival.
   - _Status_: **Unblocked / Ready to Build**.

2. **Ruling 2 (`WF-37`) — Reconcile Booked Transport on Bed Cancellation [APPROVED]**:
   - _Ruling_: **Yes** — bed cancellation automatically cancels or prompts cancellation of linked booked transport.
   - _Clinical Rationale_: Releasing or cancelling a bed reservation (`RELEASE_PULL`) must automatically cancel or prompt the cancellation of any linked booked transport job, preventing wasted ambulance/crew journeys and avoiding patient arrival at a ward with zero capacity.
   - _Status_: **Unblocked / Ready to Build**.

3. **Ruling 3 (`WF-01`, `WF-10`) — Involuntary Bed Matching & Zero Vacant Locked Bed Handling [CRITICAL CLINICAL RULING]**:
   - _Ruling_: **NO to rejecting wards with 0 vacant locked beds. Patients often need to be waitlisted!**
   - _Clinical Rationale_: Rejecting or hiding wards from referral matching simply because they have zero currently unoccupied locked beds is clinically incorrect. Patients requiring secure/locked care frequently need to be waitlisted for a bed in their home catchment rather than immediately diverted out of catchment or rejected. Full wards must remain selectable so coordinators can queue referrals, hold priority candidates, and manage bed flow as discharges occur.
   - _Prioritisation Hierarchy_:
     1. **1st: In catchment capacity** — Wards in the patient's home catchment that currently have available beds.
     2. **2nd: Available with beds** — Alternative out-of-catchment or general services that have available beds.
     3. **3rd: In catchment with no beds** — Wards in the patient's home catchment with zero current vacancies (waitlist queue / hold placement).
   - _Status_: **Architectural & Clinical Baseline Updated**. Overrides prior proposals to auto-reject 0-vacancy wards.

4. **Ruling 4 (`WF-13`, `P3-WD`) — Terminal State on Referral Withdrawal [APPROVED]**:
   - _Ruling_: **Yes** — terminal state on referral withdrawal.
   - _Clinical Rationale_: `WITHDRAW_REFERRAL` must be a definitive terminal state that removes the referral from active triage queues and prevents subsequent operational events. On post-acceptance withdrawals (`P3-WD`), the engine must cleanly reset `acceptedUnitId` (or void the accepted assignment) so withdrawn records do not display an accepted ward name or retain residual allocations.
   - _Status_: **Unblocked / Ready to Build**.

5. **Ruling 5 (`D-14`) — Network-Wide Search Privacy & Cross-Ward Referral Lookup [LEFT FOR NOW]**:
   - _Ruling_: **Leave network-wide search privacy for now** — building full system; future role-based logins will govern access.
   - _Clinical Rationale_: We are building out the complete integrated prototype across all screens and services. Restrictive default-deny cross-ward referral lookups and premature privacy gates should not obstruct coordinator workflow across screens while prototyping. Granular clinical access boundaries, tenant isolation, and visibility rules will be governed by future role-based logins when production authentication is introduced.
   - _Status_: **Deferred to Production Auth Phase**. No restrictive cross-ward default-deny blocking applied in prototype.

6. **Ruling 6 (`ISSUE-P2-88`) — Community Screen Transport Booking Affordance [APPROVED]**:
   - _Ruling_: **Yes** — community screen transport booking affordance (link admission to movement).
   - _Clinical Rationale_: Link `Admission` to `Movement` so the community mental health screen's "bed pulled" list has a direct `movementId`, enabling community clinicians to access transport booking and cancellation affordances (`BOOK_TRANSPORT`, `CANCEL_TRANSPORT`) directly.
   - _Status_: **Unblocked / Ready to Build**.

7. **Ruling 7 (`DRAWER-RESPONSIVE-SHEET`) — Mobile Drawer Responsive Sheet [APPROVED]**:
   - _Ruling_: **Approved** — responsive mobile drawer sheet.
   - _Clinical Rationale_: Convert right-hand 48rem sheet to a 100vw full-screen or bottom-sheet drawer with swipe-to-dismiss gesture on small mobile viewports (<640px) to prevent horizontal clipping.
   - _Status_: **Approved for Implementation**.

8. **Ruling 8 (`P2` Touch Ergonomics) — 44px Minimum Tap Targets [APPROVED]**:
   - _Ruling_: **Approved** — 44px tap targets.
   - _Clinical Rationale_: Enforce minimum 44×44px interactive tap target sizes across mobile/tablet interfaces, including the raise-referral trigger and compact table actions, preventing misclicks on touch devices.
   - _Status_: **Approved for Implementation**.

9. **Ruling 9 (`ED-FORM-DROPDOWN` / `ED-FORM-POPOVER`) — ED Inline Legal Form Dropdown [APPROVED]**:
   - _Ruling_: **Approved** — ED inline legal form dropdown.
   - _Clinical Rationale_: Implement the Third-Edition mockup's interactive listbox dropdown (`data-form-open`, `aria-haspopup="listbox"`, `ul.formMenu`) on the `.formPill` element in `ed-screen.tsx`, allowing clinicians to change or assign statutory forms directly from the board row with 1:1 mockup parity.
   - _Status_: **Approved for Implementation**.

10. **Ruling 10 — Patient Journey Accordion Keyboard Accessibility [APPROVED]**:
    - _Ruling_: **Approved** — journey accordion keyboard accessibility.
    - _Clinical Rationale_: Ensure complete keyboard accessibility (Enter/Space toggling, `aria-expanded`, visible focus outlines, roving tabindex) for the interactive 7-stage presentation bedflow milestone accordion on the patient details screen.
    - _Status_: **Approved for Implementation**.

11. **Ruling 11 (`SOVEREIGN-KEYBOARD-BUS`) — Global Drawer Keyboard Shortcuts [APPROVED]**:
    - _Ruling_: **Approved** — drawer shortcuts.
    - _Clinical Rationale_: Wire global access keys (`Shift+T` for Tasks, `Shift+R` for Referral Drawer, `Shift+A` for Activity) with standard Escape dismissal and focus restoration for rapid keyboard navigation.
    - _Status_: **Approved for Implementation**.

12. **Ruling 12 (`REFERRAL-DRAWER-CAPACITY`) — Referral Drawer Live Capacity Binding [APPROVED]**:
    - _Ruling_: **Approved** — referral drawer live capacity.
    - _Clinical Rationale_: Bind Stage 3 of `ward-referral-drawer.tsx` dynamically to live network capacity records (`useWardCapacity()` / `CAPACITY_RECORDS`), ensuring unit suggestions accurately reflect real-time male/female acute bed availability across all services.
    - _Status_: **Approved for Implementation**.

13. **Ruling 13 — Community Contact Information Unlinked Until Verified [APPROVED]**:
    - _Ruling_: **Approved** — leave community contact unlinked until verified.
    - _Clinical Rationale_: Community mental health contact phone numbers and email links remain plain unlinked text until verified against genuine WA health service directories, preventing misdialing of dummy numbers.
    - _Status_: **Enforced as Standard**.

14. **Ruling 14 — Playwright Browser Journey Test Prompt [APPROVED / CREATED]**:
    - _Ruling_: **Approved** — browser journey test prompt created for running in a separate chat.
    - _Clinical Rationale_: The 12 browser-level Playwright specs (`npm run test:e2e:ward-journeys`) are packaged into an isolated prompt for dedicated execution against the running local server, avoiding test-runner load collisions in development sessions.
    - _Status_: **Documented & Staged for Dedicated Run**.

15. **Ruling 15 (`VISUAL-SNAPSHOTS-SOVEREIGN`, `ED-VISUAL-SNAPSHOTS`) — Visual Regression Baseline Capture [APPROVED]**:
    - _Ruling_: **Approved** — visual snapshots baseline capture.
    - _Clinical Rationale_: Capture Playwright visual regression baselines (`npm run test:visual`) for both the ED screen at 1440px desktop breakpoint and the third-edition sovereign rail in open (264px) and collapsed (72px) states across themes.
    - _Status_: **Approved for Execution**.

16. **Ruling 16 — Design System Documentation Update [APPROVED]**:
    - _Ruling_: **Approved** — update design system document to 34 screens + standing rules, noting ongoing design perfection.
    - _Clinical Rationale_: Update `docs/ward-flow/mockups/WARD-FLOW-DESIGN-SYSTEM.md` to reflect the full active 34-screen estate and standing rules (Rule D5: no calculated statutory countdowns, clinician-typed expiries, independent ED clocks, manual bed release), explicitly noting ongoing design perfection across the screen estate.
    - _Status_: **Approved for Documentation Update**.

17. **Ruling 17 — Aboriginal Cultural Safety Review [DEFERRED]**:
    - _Ruling_: **Defer** — cultural safety review stays deferred.
    - _Clinical Rationale_: Stays deferred on the task ledger per owner instruction. Retained as a mandatory pre-clinical governance hard gate before any real-patient use.
    - _Status_: **Deferred (Mandatory Pre-Clinical Gate)**.

18. **Ruling 18 — Outside Compliance and Governance Gates [DEFERRED]**:
    - _Ruling_: **Defer** — outside compliance/governance gates stay deferred.
    - _Clinical Rationale_: External statutory reviews (TGA medical device classification, Clinical Safety Officer sign-off, Privacy Officer review, WA legal advice, formal catchment sign-off) remain deferred during the prototype phase; recorded as high-priority production release gates.
    - _Status_: **Deferred (Production Gate)**.

---

### 2. Added Instruction: Dedicated Patient Transport Section

- **Requirement**: Build a dedicated patient transport section flagging booked transport, marking transport as booked, entering CAD transport number, and recording quoted ETA.
- **Clinical Rationale**: Extends the 17 September transport booking ruling (booking in Ward Flow logs a phone booking made to transport dispatch; it is never an automatic in-app vehicle dispatch). The dedicated section will provide clinicians and coordinators with:
  1. A clear overview of all patients with flagged or booked transport across the network.
  2. An explicit action to mark transport as booked when the telephone booking is confirmed.
  3. Input fields to record the official Computer-Aided Dispatch (CAD) booking reference number.
  4. Quoted estimated time of arrival (ETA) logging to synchronize ED offloads, ward admissions, and escort readiness.
- **Status**: **New Priority Task Queued for Implementation**.

#### §7.18.17 Three owner instructions carried out, and one carried out narrower than asked

**Committed `e770aae1ab`, 2026-09-18, verified in a browser rather than only by test.**

✅ **Q-12 AMENDED BY THE OWNER, and this is the part to read before re-opening anything above.**
Offered the choice between restoring "Worth your attention" as its own panel and keeping the
third-edition rebuild's "Attention" tab, **he chose the tab.** So Q-12 — _"keep every one, drop
nothing… folded into the nearest drawn panel, or into the Activity drawer"_ (2026-09-12) — is
satisfied here by the item being **present, reachable, and showing its count**, not by it being a
`region`. That is a fold into a drawn panel, which the ruling's own wording allows. 🔴 **The panel
form is not coming back; do not raise it again.** The restored citation in
`ward-delays-third-edition.dom.test.tsx` says so in the test itself.

✅ **The provenance panel is back** and renders live with derived figures — "2 escalations and 7
people resolved today", "10 emergency departments … 5 health services". That 10 is itself a
cross-check on the reference work: 8 EDs plus KEMH and PCH.

🔴 **THE BOARD INSTRUCTION WAS CARRIED OUT NARROWER THAN GIVEN, DELIBERATELY.** The owner asked for
the 64 real catchment teams to be wired into the ward board's "Where these beds free up to" panel,
replacing ten names each ending "(placeholder)". **Wiring the real names in would have been worse
than leaving the stubs**, and the reason is decided elsewhere rather than by judgement here:
`arrowTargets` buckets by `admission.homeRegion`, so a team named there is **derived from where the
patient lives** — and owner decision 8 (2026-09-17, answer 20) is _"referral lists narrow by sending
and receiving service, never home area"_, with the research pack forbidding the same move in its own
words: never match on suburb, postcode, LGA or proximity.

⚠️ **The asymmetry is the whole argument: "(placeholder)" reads on sight as a stand-in;
"Butler Community Mental Health Service" reads as a fact.** The swap would have converted an obvious
stub into a false routing claim about a real service. ✅ **Nothing records a team for these people**
— `Admission` (`ward-admissions.ts:288`) carries no team field, and `bookedBy.placeId` identifies
the community team that booked TRANSPORT, a different fact about a different act. So the panel now
states the absence in the words it already used for that case. Verified live: **zero "(placeholder)"
strings on the board, eight regions each saying no team is recorded.**

**The real fix, when the owner wants it:** record a team against the referral or admission, then read
THAT. It is a model change, not a name swap. Raised, not started.

## §7.19 — A RULING ENFORCED IN FOUR PLACES OUT OF FIVE (2026-09-18)

**Owner-approved clinical fix. Branch `claude/ward-form-codes-20260918`, commits `4c16192b7f` and
`918f7e24db`, off `10c4ce9e1c`.**

The Chief Psychiatrist register titles **Form 5A "Community Treatment Order"** — an order for
treatment **in the community** — and **Form 6A "Inpatient treatment order in authorised hospital"**
(`src/lib/form-register.ts:69`, `:84`).

`ward-index.tsx` gave **all 24 ward entries plus the unknown-ward fallback** `mhaForms` lines naming
**Form 5A** as an authority that unit holds people under. Every entry is a locked HDU, a
psychogeriatric unit, a forensic secure unit, a perinatal secure unit or an open inpatient ward.
**Not one is a community team.** It renders on the ward profile as "WA MHA 2014 · {forms}". Swapped
to Form 6A. The owner approved the swap on 2026-09-18.

`handover-page.tsx` captioned its "Recorded form timings" panel **"Form 1A / Form 4A / Form 5A"**
while the panel below renders only 1A and 4A items — a caption naming a form its own panel never
shows. Dropped to "Form 1A / Form 4A".

### 🔴 THE SHAPE IS THE LESSON, NOT THE FORM CODE

**This ruling already existed, in writing, and everyone believed it was enforced.**
`ward-legal-figure-guard.test.ts:3177` records it verbatim — _"Form 5A is a Community Treatment
Order in the register, never an involuntary inpatient status"_ — and **three screens each grew
their own local guard forbidding the string**: patient search, settings, the movement horizon. The
live screens were correct. It read as closed.

⚠️ **The ward register had no guard, because nobody had looked at the ward register.** So a rule
believed enforced was enforced in four places out of five, and **26 instances** sat behind it
untouched. The drawings carried it too — including the _identical_ handover caption, which is where
the screen's caption came from.

🔴 **A per-instance guard's population is the file it sits in.** N guards enforce the ruling on N
files and say nothing whatever about file N+1 — but N guards accumulating over weeks READ as
coverage. Every individual fix here was right and every guard was real. What was never asked was
_how many_, and one tree-wide `grep -c` answered it in a second.

✅ **The generalising fix, and the pattern to copy.** `tests/ward-register-inpatient-form-codes.test.ts`
keys on the register's own **`category === "Community treatment orders"`** rather than on the
literal `"5A"`, so a new community form code inherits the guard without anyone remembering. It
carries anti-vacuity floors at **both** ends — at least 4 community codes must exist to look for,
at least 20 ward entries must exist to look at, and at least 20 must name Form 6A — so neither
emptying the register nor renaming the category can pass by measuring nothing.

**When a ruling is found violated anywhere: enumerate the whole population BEFORE fixing any of it,
drawings and fixtures included. Then write one guard keyed on what the ruling is about.**

### Left open, deliberately — clinical calls, not register lookups

- **"Form 5A Return"** in `movement-third-edition.html` and **"WF-214 · Form 5A Leave (+14h)"** in
  the gantt. Leave and return under a community order is either a real arrangement or a muddle.
  **For the owner.**
- Untouched and correct: _"Form 5A community treatment order breached. Involuntary admission …"_ —
  a breached CTO leading to admission is a real pathway — the `legalStatus: "Form 5A"` fixture rows
  behind it, and `<option value="Form 5A">` where the code is the value rather than a label.
- `docs/ward-flow/mockups/reference/gemini-2026-09-15/` left alone: correcting a dated snapshot
  destroys the record of what that tool actually said.
- `SCREEN-VERIFICATION.md` now marks `patient-search-third-edition.html` 🔴 STALE. **Correct, and
  left standing** — the change is one word in one option label, but nobody has LOOKED at the drawing
  beside its screen since, and that record exists precisely because looking is the only instrument.

### Two reds investigated — one flake, one real and fixed. Neither is from this work.

🔴 **THIS HEADING SAID "Two pre-existing reds on the ward line" UNTIL 2026-09-18, AND THE BODY
BELOW WAS CORRECTED WHILE THE HEADING WAS NOT.** The other session caught it and declined to fix it
itself, because this file was being folded around and a second committer would have been the larger
problem.

⚠️ **It is the same defect this section is about, one level up.** The correction was made
in the place somebody was looking, and the summary above it kept the old claim — and a heading is
what a skimming reader takes away. "Two reds on the line" is the exact wrong impression, and it is
the impression that sends the next session hunting a bug that is not there. **A correction that does
not reach the summary has not been made.**

Both failed identically at `10c4ce9e1c` with none of these commits applied, and neither is a red
now:

- `ward-legal-figure-guard.test.ts` — timed out at 90 s on its combinatorial `dueAt` sweep (115 s
  of test time). It imports neither file changed here.

  🔴 **AMENDED 2026-09-18 BY WARD LEAD, WITH THE OTHER SESSION'S AGREEMENT: THIS IS NOT A
  RED.** It was observed exceeding 90 s twice — once alongside four other files and once alone
  — and passing cleanly on the same tree on a third run, 16 tests green. **It has never failed an
  assertion.** Load-dependent timeout fits all three observations; a defect fits none of them.
  ⚠️ **Leaving "pre-existing red" standing here would have been worse than saying nothing**,
  because a red nobody can reproduce sends the next session hunting a bug that is not there — and
  it is the third guard this night to be red, green, then red across runs without an assertion ever
  failing. Amended here rather than by a second session pushing at a file being folded around.

- `ward-screen-overview-and-entry.dom.test.tsx` — cannot find the "All Beds (20)" button. Recorded
  as a **lead, not a finding**, because it was never bisected: the test pins a bed total that "five
  wards resized to sample numbers" (`10c4ce9e1c`) changed.

  ✅ **THE LEAD WAS RIGHT, AND THE CAUTION WAS STILL CORRECT.** Confirmed and fixed at
  `625202ce9d`: `tests/ward-screen-overview-and-entry.dom.test.tsx:222` pinned the literal
  `"All Beds (20)"`, and resizing `rph-adult-secure` 20 → 15 broke a whole DOM suite on one typed
  number while the screen itself was correct. It now reads `unitById("rph-adult-secure")?.beds`,
  with a non-vacuity guard above it. ⚠️ **That is not an expectation derived from its own
  subject:** the subject is the SCREEN — does it label the matrix with the ward's bed count —
  and the fixture is the independent source it must agree with, so a screen showing any other number
  still fails.

#### §7.18.18 Four questions the owner had already answered, and why nothing caught it

**2026-09-18.** The community-team mapping document put six decisions to the owner. 🔴 **Four of them
he had ruled on thirteen days earlier, on 2026-09-05** — the four `Inner City` spellings, recorded
in `community-ratified-aliases.ts` with his question, his answer and the suburb counts he was shown
all sitting in the table. I drafted around that table without reading it.

🔴 **HIS ANSWER TODAY MATCHED HIS ANSWER THEN, AND THAT IS EXACTLY WHY IT GOES UNNOTICED.** A
re-answered question and a newly-answered one are **indistinguishable in the outcome**: question
asked, answer returned, answer correct, work proceeds. ⚠️ **Had the two answers disagreed, either
session would have investigated within a minute. The agreement is what buries it.**

⚠️ **And the cost lands entirely on him and leaves no artefact.** No gate fails, no test reddens, no
count moves. The only evidence is the owner's time, which nothing in this process measures — so
unlike every other class of defect recorded in this ledger, this one has no instrument at all.

✅ **The remedy, generalised past team names:** treat _"is this actually open?"_ as a verification
step held to the same standard as any other claim. Before a question is drafted, grep the ratified
tables, `owner-decisions-*.md`, `owner-answers-*.md` and the relevant guards' expectation lines **for
the exact subject**. **When several questions go at once, check each separately** — they were opened
at different times by different work, and one being genuinely open says nothing about its
neighbours. ✅ **State in the message where you looked**, so the next person inherits the search
instead of repeating it, and so a search in the wrong place is visible.

⚠️ **This is the sibling of the 2026-09-01 lesson about assigned WORK** (an assignment is a statement
about the past, not evidence the work is outstanding). **Same shape, different object, and neither
covers the other:** that one costs an agent's effort and is eventually caught by the work already
existing; this one costs the owner's attention and is caught by nothing.

**What the owner's ruling then did buy us:** the alias group is now READ rather than retyped.
`community-team-contact-mapping.ts` names one member and the other three resolve through his
2026-09-05 table, verified live — `/community/icc` reaches City East with no row of its own.
Repeating the four spellings would have been a second home for a ruling that already had one.

#### §7.19.1 The ward line does not typecheck, and I told the owner otherwise

🔴 **CORRECTION, 2026-09-19, caught by the peer session.** I reported the 10 typecheck errors to the
owner twice as sitting "in work nobody has finished writing" — implying they would vanish when their
author finished, and that nothing had been committed. **They are committed to
`codex/task-ward-flow-live-state-20260831`.** All four files are clean in `git status`; the peer
reproduced every error in a fresh checkout of `016e75950c` with no working tree of its own.

⚠️ **THIS IS THE EXPIRED-OBSERVATION FAILURE AGAIN, AND THE THIRD TIME TODAY.** The framing was true
when I first wrote it — the errors WERE in uncommitted files. **Then I committed those files myself,
in the sixth protective snapshot, and went on describing them with the pre-snapshot framing.** My own
action invalidated my own statement and I did not re-check it. See §7.18.12.

**What it changes, and why it is not pedantry:** the ward line does not compile. **Anyone branching
from it inherits red.** And it is no longer recoverable by its author simply not saving — removing it
is now a decision rather than a discard.

**The ten, all from `7f64c1a4ac4` (2026-09-18), one unfinished priority-tier feature:**
`ward-board.tsx` ×4 (`who` absent on `ShiftTileItem`), `ward-referrals.ts` ×4 (`pulledAt` absent on
`Movement`, `stage` absent on `TransportJob`), `ward-shortlist-candidates.test.ts` ×1 (a `waitlist`
availability the enum does not carry), and one worth reading on its own:

🔴 **`ward-derivations.ts:931` — a legal-status comparison that can NEVER be true.**
`movement.legalStatus === "Involuntary"`, where `LegalStatus` is `"Voluntary" | "Referred for
psychiatric examination" | "Detained awaiting examination" | "Involuntary inpatient"`. **There is no
`"Involuntary"`.** So `const isInvoluntary = movement.legalStatus === "Involuntary" ||
movement.security === "Secure"` collapses to the security check alone, and at `:1000` that flag is
what applies priority-tier ordering to the bed shortlist. **An involuntary inpatient not separately
marked Secure is ranked without it.**

✅ **NOT FIXED, deliberately, and the reason is the blame line rather than caution.** Both the
comparison and the tier feature it feeds come from the SAME commit, today. This is unfinished work,
not a defect in shipped logic — so `"Involuntary inpatient"` would not be a typo correction, it would
**switch on tier ordering for a population that has never had it, in a feature its author has not
finished.** Changing which beds are offered first to a detained patient is not a merge-hygiene
decision. ⚠️ **Recorded for the owner and its author; the fix is one string and the consequence is
clinical.**

**Also corrected, from the same message:** my merge commit's reasoning about `referrals.module.css`
said the branch dropped a `var(--text-heading, var(--ink))` fallback. **That file has no such
fallback on any side** — the one I found is `ward.module.css:90`, present in all three sides and
intact. The resolution was right and the evidence for it was not. ⚠️ **A right answer reached through
a wrong premise is still a wrong premise, and it is the kind that survives because the outcome
looks fine.**

#### §7.19.2 One expiry mechanism, three severities — including a baseline that expired mid-use

**The fourth-screen fix is home** (`fe20efa80e`, folded after checking its 3 files overlapped zero of
the 6 dirty ones with both lists non-empty). `ward-ready-figure-preparation-qualifier` passes, 3
tests. **Four screens now carry the owner's 2026-09-05 sentence, from one source, copied verbatim
rather than reworded.**

🔴 **BUT THE TYPECHECK COMPARISON AFTER IT FAILED IN A NEW WAY, AND IT IS THE SHARPEST VERSION OF
TONIGHT'S RECURRING FAILURE.** Against the captured baseline the merge showed **2 new errors and 4
gone** — which reads as a merge that changed something. It did not. **All six are in
`ward-board.tsx`, a file this merge does not touch** (`git diff --name-only 016e75950c..HEAD` has
zero matches for it), which is dirty and was written at **00:38:54**, one minute before the
comparison ran. The six errors outside it are byte-for-byte the baseline's.

⚠️ **The baseline expired WHILE IT WAS BEING USED.** Not before, not after — during. A baseline is an
observation, and every observation here has a shelf life; this one's was shorter than the operation
it existed to measure.

**Stated once, as ONE mechanism with three severities, because it has now caught two sessions four
times in a night and each of us filed it separately:**

1. **A reading of someone else's state goes stale** — the mtime survey that said a tool was "actively
   writing" and was seven hours old (§7.18.12); a dirty-file list three commits old that nearly
   authorised a destructive revert.
2. 🔴 **A reading of a state YOU changed goes stale** (§7.19.1). Strictly worse: there is no external
   cause to notice, because the invalidating event was your own hand.
3. 🔴 **A baseline expires between capture and comparison.** Worst of the three, because the artefact
   still exists, still parses, still produces a diff — and the diff is _about a different tree_.

⚠️ **WHAT MAKES ALL THREE INVISIBLE IS THE SAME THING: every list was TRUE when taken, so nothing
about the artefact changes when it stops being true.** There is no corrupted file, no failing parse,
no warning. The only defence is to re-establish the fact at the moment of use rather than at the
moment of convenience — and, where a comparison is the point, **to check that the files it names were
actually in scope before believing what it says about them.**

#### §7.19.3 The journey-tooling branch: verified foldable, blocked by permission, NOT folded

**10 commits on `ward/journey-tooling-20260918` (tip `4df6d4e8cb`, last commit 00:33 on 2026-09-19)
are not in the ward line.** The work is substantial — a refusal-coverage measure, and _"the first
real refusal measurement — 30 of 313 refusals no test reaches"_.

✅ **Everything needed to fold it was verified, and it is recorded here so nobody re-does the check:**

- **It is an explicit handover, not a guess.** `~/.claude/worktree-ownership.md` carries the owning
  session's own words: _"Whoever folds it: the change is additive — one new folder plus two
  package.json script lines and five .prettierignore lines — so it should merge without a
  decision."_
- 🔴 **Both its stated blockers have CLEARED.** It needed `ward/audit-and-real-data-20260918`, now an
  ancestor of the line; and it needed `ward-lead` not to be holding another tool's uncommitted work,
  which successive protective snapshots closed. ⚠️ **Fourth deferral tonight whose stated condition
  quietly cleared while the deferral stood — a deferral records a reason, and then nothing ever
  re-reads the reason.** Same mechanism as §7.19.2's three expiry severities, pointed at a decision
  rather than a measurement, with a longer fuse.
- **Its scope is unchanged despite ten further commits:** 29 files under `docs/ward-flow/journey/`,
  plus `package.json` and `.prettierignore`. Nothing outside. `docs/ward-flow/journey` does not exist
  on this line at all, so it is additive in fact rather than only in description.
- **Zero overlap with the 10 files dirty here, both lists non-empty.** `merge-tree` exit 0.

🔴 **AND THE POINT THAT SETTLED THE SAFETY QUESTION, WHICH TWO SESSIONS HAD WRONG.** Both this
session and the peer declined to fold because the branch's worktree is clean with a commit thirty
minutes old — a session at a pause, and "the one thing that would break its work is the branch moving
underneath it". ✅ **But merging a branch INTO the ward line does not move that branch.** The owning
worktree stays on `ward/journey-tooling-20260918` at `4df6d4e8cb`, untouched; it can keep committing,
and a later fold takes the rest. **The hazard we were both protecting against is not a hazard this
operation has** — we had generalised a real rule (do not move another session's branch) onto an
operation that does not do that.

⚠️ **NOT FOLDED: the session's auto-mode classifier refused the merge** ("Modify Shared Resources"),
as it refused the throwaway-worktree removal earlier (§7.18.15). **No override was attempted.** It
needs the owner's hand or a permission rule. Nothing is at risk — the branch is committed and backed
up — but the work stays out of the line until somebody with the permission folds it.

#### §7.19.4 The journey tooling is home — and its measurement was irreproducible BY CONSTRUCTION, because vitest deletes the report after a failing run

**Josh folded `ward/journey-tooling-20260918` himself at `8c97d41ce8`** after this session's
classifier refused the merge. 10 commits, 29 files under `docs/ward-flow/journey/`, clean.

✅ **Verified by its own instrument, which is what its owning session asked whoever folded it to
do.** `npm run ward:journey` exit 0; `npm run ward:journey:prove` exit 0 — and the prove run is the
good kind, **showing each check failing on a deliberately broken input**: a report with no source
named is refused, a report measured against a different reducer is refused, a report with no lines
for the reducer is refused rather than read as "nothing is tested", and a line the report never
mentions is **unknown, not untested**.

🔴 **BUT RUNNING IT MOVED THE HEADLINE FIGURE, AND THE CAUSE IS WORTH MORE THAN THE FIGURE.**
`refusal-coverage.json` went from `spokenFor: 313` to `spokenFor: 129` — the measure can now speak
for 129 of 313 refusals, 97 of those never reached by a test, and **184 reported as unknown rather
than untested**.

⚠️ **Nothing regressed. The INPUT changed.** The measure reads `coverage/lcov.info`, and that file
is **untracked** — a local artefact of whenever tests last happened to run with coverage on this
disk (23:55 tonight). **So a committed output depends on an uncommitted input.** Two people running
`ward:journey` on the same commit get different numbers, and neither is wrong; the committed file is
a record of somebody's local state rather than a property of the code.

✅ **The tool itself is not at fault and is in fact unusually careful** — it refuses a report it
cannot trust and never calls an unmeasured line untested, which is why the drop presents as 184
UNKNOWN rather than as 184 newly-untested refusals. **A less careful tool would have reported a
catastrophe.**

**For the owner, and it is a small decision with a long tail:** either the coverage report becomes a
tracked, regenerated input (so the number means the same thing to everybody), or the committed
`refusal-coverage.json` carries its input's identity and date so a reader knows which local run it
describes. **"30 of 313 refusals no test reaches" is a quotable sentence, and right now it is
quotable without being reproducible.**

The regenerated outputs are committed rather than reverted, because leaving them would make the
committed files disagree with what the generator produces on this tree — generated-file drift, which
this repository has its own gates against.

#### §7.19.5 Two dots and three dots — a well-formed answer to the wrong question

**The last journey-tooling commit is home** (`01a669ac44`, folding `f178e3c383`, on Josh's
instruction). ✅ **And it confirms the thing two sessions had wrong earlier: a merge FROM a branch
does not move it.** `ward/journey-tooling-20260918` still points at `f178e3c383` after the fold, so
the session working there sees nothing change underneath it. **"Do not fold another chat's branch"
is usually stated without that clause, and the clause is what makes it actionable.**

🔴 **THE NEAR-MISS ON THE WAY THERE IS A NEW SHAPE, AND IT IS NOT AN EXPIRY.** The peer's first
overlap check used `git diff --name-only HEAD ward/journey-tooling-20260918` — **two dots, between
two diverged tips.** Because the ward line is 65 commits ahead, that reports the symmetric
difference of BOTH directions: ~170 files, including `ward-board.tsx`, `ward-flow-reducer.ts`,
`ward-model.ts` and `ward-referrals.ts`. **Twelve overlapped the dirty list.**

⚠️ **It looked exactly like a merge about to collide with in-flight work, and every number in it was
real.** The output was not wrong. **It answered a question nobody had asked.** What a merge WRITES is
`HEAD...branch` — three dots, changes on the branch since the merge base — which was **one file and
zero overlap**.

🔴 **This fails in the more dangerous direction than the expiry family.** §7.19.2's three severities
all manufacture false COMFORT or a false finding from stale data; this manufactures **ALARM** from
fresh data. It would have stopped a safe fold and produced twelve fabricated conflicts to report.
**And unlike an expiry it does not go stale — it is wrong the moment it is typed and stays wrong**,
so no amount of re-measuring catches it.

✅ **The tell, and it is memorable: a diff between two diverged branches is SYMMETRIC and a merge is
not.**

- `HEAD...branch` (three dots) — what the merge brings. Use this for overlap, scope and conflict
  prediction.
- `HEAD..branch` (two dots) — which commits are on the branch and not here. Use this for counting.
- `git diff HEAD branch` (two dots between tips) — the symmetric difference. **Almost never the
  question, and the more the two have diverged the more convincingly wrong it looks.**

This session used three dots for its own overlap checks tonight and got one file, which is why the
journey fold looked safe here and alarming there — **the same tree, the same moment, two commands.**
