## §7.19.6 — TWO OWNER RULINGS ON INVENTED AND INAPPLICABLE NUMBERS (2026-09-19)

Both put to the owner together, deliberately, because they are the same shape: **a number that
looks derived, is presented without qualification, and is either invented or silently never
applies.** He ruled on both in one line: _"no split, and no priority ordering for now."_

### Ruling 1 — the male/female split of free beds: REMOVED

`useWardCapacity()` in `ward-referral-drawer.tsx` derived `maleReady = Math.ceil(cap.available / 2)`
and `femaleReady = Math.floor(...)`. **That asserts a sex split of free beds that no data supports**,
on the panel a coordinator uses to choose a bed for a specific person.

✅ **It was never rendered and no test read it** — 42 occurrences across the interface, eight fixture
rows and the derivation, consumed by nothing. So nobody was misled, and **that is exactly why it had
to go rather than stay**: it sat among figures that ARE genuinely derived (`readyBeds` reads real
`unitCapacity`), so whoever next built that panel would have wired it up in good faith and shipped a
fabricated split without ever learning it was fabricated. A field with no consumer is not harmless;
it is a trap with a delay on it.

🔴 **If a real split is ever wanted it needs per-ward occupancy this prototype does not have.** And
the question a coordinator actually has — _can this ward take THIS person_ — is already answered per
ward by the eligibility check against `sexDesignation` and `sexMix`. A headline split adds nothing to
that and invites a false read. `Unit.sexMix` exists but `ward-model.ts:494` records that it, like
every number on a unit, is invented.

### Ruling 2 — catchment priority ordering for detained patients: NOT NOW

🔴 **DO NOT "FIX" THE COMPARISON AT `ward-derivations.ts:931`. IT LOOKS LIKE A ONE-WORD TYPO AND IT
IS A CLINICAL DECISION.**

`shortlistCandidates()` reads
`const isInvoluntary = movement.legalStatus === "Involuntary" || movement.security === "Secure";`
and `LegalStatus` is `"Voluntary" | "Referred for psychiatric examination" | "Detained awaiting
examination" | "Involuntary inpatient"`. **There is no `"Involuntary"`**, so that half can never be
true and `isInvoluntary` collapses to the security check. Its only consumer is the `.sort()` at
`:1000`, which applies tier ordering — tier 1 in-catchment with a bed, tier 2 out-of-catchment with
a bed, **tier 3 in-catchment with NO bed, kept on the list as a waitlist option rather than dropped**,
tier 4 the rest. WF-01's own comment says that tier 3 behaviour exists _for involuntary placements_.

⚠️ **So the feature's stated purpose — keep a detained patient's home ward visible even when it has
no bed today — currently never applies to anyone who is not separately flagged as needing a secure
bed.** Changing `"Involuntary"` to `"Involuntary inpatient"` would not correct a typo; it would
**switch catchment-priority ordering on for a population that has never had it**, inside a feature
its author has not finished. The recommendation put to the owner was the two statuses where the
person cannot leave — _detained awaiting examination_ and _involuntary inpatient_ — with _referred
for psychiatric examination_ named as the genuine borderline.

**He ruled no priority ordering for now.** So the current behaviour stands and nothing was changed.

🔴 **`ward-derivations.ts` WAS NOT EDITED, and not out of caution: it was dirty and mid-edit by its
author while this ruling was recorded.** The dead comparison is also one of the ten typecheck errors
standing on this line. Whoever finishes that feature owns both the cleanup and this decision —
**and if the ordering is wanted later, the question to bring back is "which legal statuses", never
"should this string match".**

#### §7.19.7 The coverage report deletes itself after a failing run — four runs measured nothing

🔴 **CORRECTION TO §7.19.4, FROM THE JOURNEY TOOLING'S ACTUAL OWNER (`ward-lead-6a`), AND IT IS A
BETTER DIAGNOSIS THAN MINE.** I wrote that the refusal figure "is not reproducible", which reads as
doubt about the figure. **The figure was sound for its input.** What was irreproducible is the
ARTEFACT — and the cause is not merely that `coverage/lcov.info` is untracked.

**`vitest`'s `coverage.reportOnFailure` DEFAULTS TO FALSE, and on a run with any failing test it
writes the coverage report and then DELETES THE DIRECTORY AGAIN.** Verified here in this version's
own source rather than taken on report: `reportOnFailure: false` in
`node_modules/vitest/dist/chunks/defaults.*.js`, and
`if (!this.options.reportOnFailure) await this.cleanAfterRun()` in `coverage.*.js`.

⚠️ **THE RUN LOOKS IDENTICAL WHETHER IT MEASURED EVERYTHING OR NOTHING.** `npm run test:coverage`
prints "Coverage enabled with v8", runs for half an hour, prints an entirely ordinary summary, exits
— and leaves no `coverage/` directory. **Three of the owner's runs and one of this session's
measured nothing that way**, and mine was the 27-minute run tonight that failed 59 tests and wrote
no report at all. Nobody caught it by reading output; it was caught by stat-ing the directory.

🔴 **SO THE `regenerateWith` LINE I ADDED IN §7.19.4 DID NOT REGENERATE ANYTHING.** Corrected to
`npm run test:coverage -- --coverage.reportOnFailure=true`, with the mechanism recorded beside it,
because a regeneration instruction that silently produces no measurement is worse than none — it
converts "I have not measured this" into "I measured it and it said nothing".

✅ **The rest of §7.19.4 stands and its owner adopted it unchanged:** `reportSha256` +
`reducerSha256` + `coversWholeReducer` is the right discriminator, and stating a partial report as
normal rather than as a regression was the missing half. **The 313 → 129 drop had a mundane cause** —
a two-file probe run left its `lcov.info` behind at 23:55 and `ward:journey` read it. Two files'
worth of coverage genuinely can only speak for 129 of 313 refusals. **Neither number was wrong; they
answered different questions**, which is the whole point of recording the input.

**The current real figure, from its owner:** 30 of 313 refusals never reached, from a ward-scoped run
of 573 files. Six of the thirty were one safeguard repeated — a ward acting on another ward's bed
release, and the matching admission case — now covered by `tests/ward-acting-unit-guards.test.ts`,
12 tests, each with a control that fails if the refusal comes from anything but the wrong acting
unit.

#### §7.19.8 The refusal figure, measured properly: 24 of 312 never reached

**From the journey tooling's owner (`ward-lead-6a`), committed at `7a4c065ddf`.**

    before   313 refusals, 313 spoken for, 30 never reached
    after    312 refusals, 312 spoken for, 24 never reached

🔴 **AND THE CAVEAT IS WORTH MORE THAN THE NUMBER: the six-place fall is NOT the evidence.** The
reducer moved between the two runs — the ward line merged in and one refusal went with it, which is
why the denominator is 312 rather than 313. ⚠️ **A drop of six in a figure whose POPULATION also
changed proves nothing on its own.** It is the same shape as §7.19.5's two-dot diff: a real number
answering a question nobody asked.

✅ **The actual evidence is per-line**, each guard located by its own wording rather than by a
remembered line number — because a line number is a different number in every tree:

    REVERT_BED_RELEASE                         4717   reached, once
    BLOCK_BED_RELEASE                          4749   reached, once
    CLEAR_BED_RELEASE_BLOCK                    4795   reached, once
    SET_BED_PREPARATION                        4829   reached, once
    RELEASE_BED                                4865   reached, once
    RECORD_RETURNED_FROM_EMERGENCY_DEPARTMENT  4485   reached, once

**Once each is exactly what twelve tests over six guards should produce** — neither zero nor a number
that would suggest something else was exercising them. Sixteen acting-unit guards exist; the other
ten were already covered, so those six were the whole gap.

**The run, recorded because a figure without its run is not quotable:** ward-scoped, 581 test files,
6,616 passing, 27 failing, none of the failures in `tests/ward-acting-unit-guards.test.ts`.

🔴 **Measured against a tree that INCLUDES this session's fixture revert `2dcf4e50d5`, and that is
load-bearing.** The previous run predates it and was measuring a tree where five wards held more
occupants than beds. ⚠️ **Nothing derived from that earlier run should be trusted** — and the owner
had a coverage run queued that would have measured the broken fixture and reported the result as
fact. **The warning arrived in time only because the revert was reported rather than quietly made.**

`refusal-coverage.json` now carries `reportSha256` and `reducerSha256` (§7.19.4), so the next reader
can tell whether their own number should match this one before wondering why it does not.

#### 🔴 §7.19.9 Four artefacts that answered confidently and wrongly (2026-09-20)

Four repairs in one evening, and they are recorded together because **three of them share a shape
that is worth naming: an artefact that fails by producing a confident answer rather than by
breaking.** A broken thing gets fixed. A thing that reports a wrong verdict in the format of a right
one gets believed, and the two sessions that believed these each had to be argued out of them.

**1. Two archive notes that had been through a double encoding accident.** `24c414aac5`.
`adoption-fanout-2026-09-04.md` and `deletion-check-ui-ward-morning-2026-09-02.md` were committed at
`65c2cdacb8` carrying **14,019 NUL bytes** between them and reading as garbage. They were not lost:
the original UTF-8 had been read back as **cp437** and then saved as **UTF-16LE**, two mechanical
steps that reverse exactly. ✅ **The guilty middle codec was identified rather than guessed —
cp850 and cp1252 both raise `UnicodeEncodeError` on characters the text contains, and only cp437
round-trips.** `check-source-control-chars` exit 0, zero `U+FFFD`, zero cp437-signature characters.

⚠️ **The verification step is what nearly misled.** Printing the repaired text crashes on a
Windows cp1252 console, which reads exactly like a failed conversion. Verify by counts on disk and
by the gate's exit code, never by echoing the text.

**2. Four document links the same commit broke.** Same commit archived the dated notes without
repointing the four documents citing them. Both targets existed; only the paths were stale.
`check-doc-links` exit 0.

**3. The comparison that baited two sessions into reversing an owner ruling.** `1cfc6f4582`.
`shortlistCandidates` compared `movement.legalStatus` against `"Involuntary"` — not a member of
`LegalStatus` — behind an `as unknown as string` cast that hid it from the compiler. It could never
be true, so the expression already meant `security === "Secure"`, which is exactly the owner's
2026-09-18 ruling. **Correcting the spelling is a clinical change, not a repair:** it hands
catchment-priority ordering to involuntary inpatients, whom the owner excluded, and takes it from a
patient detained awaiting examination in a secure unit, who has it today. One population swapped for
another.

✅ **The previous defence was a comment asking people not to do it, and it had already failed
twice.** The bait is now removed rather than warned about: dead comparison and cast deleted,
behaviour unchanged, flag renamed `catchmentPriorityApplies`, and the ruling pinned by
`tests/ward-shortlist-candidates.test.ts` — secure-bed patients ARE tier-ordered, involuntary
inpatients are NOT, two non-voluntary statuses produce the identical order, with an anti-vacuity
test proving the network spans more than one tier. **Proved by mutation:** reinstating the
comparison spelled correctly reddens two assertions. 19 passed after restore; the four other files
importing `shortlistCandidates` 105 passed; `tsc -p tsconfig.typecheck.json --noEmit` **0 errors
across the project**.

**4. Four criteria in the scoring rubric that misjudge a correct screen.** `42d755ea4d`,
`docs/ward-flow/handover/02-SCORING-RUBRIC-AND-FATAL-FLAWS.md`. Originals kept; corrections beside
them and summarised at the top, because a scorer reaches the scorecard without passing the place a
correction used to sit.

- **L1 awarded 25 points for countdown timers T1/T2/T6 removed on purpose.** A correct screen scored
  badly; a reverted one scored well. Replaced with four criteria summing to 25 — register titles, no
  computed legal time, no claim of legal authority, and ⚠️ **credit for KEEPING the facts the app
  legitimately holds**, because the obvious reading of this correction is "delete the legal words"
  and that breaks the `authorisation` gate's ability to say why it refused.
- **FF4 fabricates a 0 / 200 on a working page.** `documentElement.scrollWidth > innerWidth`, while
  `globals.css` sets `overflow-x: clip` on `html` and `body`. Measured at `6fd766d37c` on
  `/mockups/ward-flow/ed/rph-ed`: documentElement **2040** in a **1425px** viewport, body **1425**
  exactly, **zero** elements past the viewport outside a scrollable ancestor. This metric produced
  the 17 September P1 against a screen already repaired, and acting on it would have dismantled a
  scroller the owner asked for.
- **CF3 scored the app against expiry windows it deliberately does not model**, and called Form 5A an
  involuntary inpatient order. 5A is a **Community Treatment Order**; the inpatient order is **6A**.
  Read from `src/lib/form-register.ts` lines 34, 59, 69, 84 rather than asserted.
- 🔴 **The scorecard template arrived already passed.** All fourteen fatal-flaw boxes ticked and
  "0 Violations (ALL CLEAR)" written in, while every other field was a blank to fill. **A pre-flight
  audit that starts at PASS can only be downgraded by someone who happens to look**, which is the
  opposite of a gate. Unticked, verdict blanked, and the note placed outside the fenced block so it
  is not pasted into every future scorecard.

⚠️ **Committed by explicit path throughout.** Another session was live in this worktree all
evening and at one point held **44 staged files including `ward-derivations.ts`**; a bare `git
commit` would have swept this work into its commit, or the reverse.

#### 🔴 7.19.10 The suite ran for the first time in four days, and 36 files were really red (2026-09-21)

**41 ward test files failed and not one was in `tests/ward-expected-reds.json`.** The last full run
was 17 September; dozens of commits from several sessions landed on top of each other in between.

✅ **36 of the 41 are real, and that number was earned rather than assumed.** Another session
had 35—50 files uncommitted in this worktree throughout, and several of the failing tests scan
source text directly, so the live tree could not answer it. The 41 were re-run on a **clean detached
checkout of the committed tree** (`D:/Worktrees/Database/suite-check-20260921` at `b9dc34a9c7`, with
`node_modules` linked by junction): **36 failed, 5 passed.** 45 failing tests out of 549, reproduced
identically across two runs.

⚠️ **Two instrument failures on the way to that number, both caught by looking rather than by
the exit code.** A run piped through `tail -60` threw away the evidence and kept only the summary. A
re-run passed `--reporter=basic`, which this vitest does not have, so it died at startup having
executed nothing — and the shell reported **exit 0**, because the exit code belonged to the pipe.
**Neither run was distinguishable from a real one by its exit status.**

## What was fixed, and the shape of what is left

✅ **Five files, two commits, re-measured on the clean tree: 36 down to 31, 45 tests down to 40,
nothing newly broken.**

- `1c4fb63a80` — **encoding damage in six shell and ward stylesheets.** The suite named ONE file
  for one stray `U+008F`. The real extent was **187 mangled runs across four files** plus five
  byte-order marks: UTF-8 read back as Windows-1252, the same accident as the two archive notes in
  7.19.9. 🔴 **And one of them was not a comment:** `ward.module.css:941` selects
  `[aria-label="Today` + chr(0x2019) + `s return"]`, its apostrophe was mangled, so the rule could never match the
  element `ward-screen.tsx` renders. It is `order: 6` — repairing it **moves a panel on the ward
  screen**, for the first time since the damage, and wants a look against the drawing.
- `0ca23adec0` — **the invented-data marker restored to the charts and the capacity provenance
  line, on the owner's decision.** A 20 September sweep removed 36 local prototype badges across
  ~20 screens in favour of one `WardPrototypeFooter` per screen. Sound for ~32 of them; not for a
  graph, where a footer says THIS SCREEN is a prototype and a badge says THIS LINE is invented.
  ⚠️ **The capacity one was broken in two steps by two commits**, which is why no single diff
  looked wrong: `f3e5de879f` took the badge out of the footer, `b9f95ae1b8` replaced the footer with
  a line that also dropped the demo-clock reading and the `aria-label` the test finds it by. The
  frozen demo clock beside live-looking bed counts is exactly what a coordinator would misread.

🔴 **THE LESSON IS NOT THE LIST. Nothing was wrong with any single change.** Each break was
invisible on the day and free to fix on the day; they became a 40-test backlog only by accumulating
unwatched. **Run the suite at the fold, not at the end of the week.**

## § 7.21 · Estate-Wide Visual, Structural & Responsive Elevation and Outstanding Triage Backlog (21 September 2026)

### § 7.21.1 Resolved in this Sprint (Commit `b02227a980`)

All 5 target P0 visual, structural, and responsive defect families across the 69 screens and modal surfaces have been resolved and verified clean:

1. **Mobile & Small Screen Adaptation**: Slide-over sheets (`width: min(100vw, 36rem)`) and bottom sheets replace rigid desktop sidebars on narrow viewports; single-row snapping tracks prevent vertical sprawl; 44px minimum tap targets across all action triggers.
2. **High-Density Desktop Layout & Spatial Hierarchy**: Sticky headers on census tables (including the 23-ward census); preserved alert tints on breaching ED cards without column overflow; accessible Card vs. Dense Table toggle (`[data-dense-view="true"]`).
3. **Dark Theme Contrast & Dual-Channel Indicators**: High-contrast borders via `:global([data-theme="dark"])` targeting `var(--line-strong)`; dual-channel glyphs (`▲`, `■`, `✓`) alongside color status indicators; 3px left indicator borders on delay cards.
4. **Tabular Numerals & Monospace Alignment**: `font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums` and `font-feature-settings: "tnum" 1` applied to all statutory countdown clocks, bed numbers, census counts, delta statistics, and telemetry readouts.
5. **Dialog Ergonomics, Focus Trapping & Scrims**: Standardized modal scrims to `var(--scrim)` with `backdrop-filter: blur(4px)`; explicit Escape listeners and focus trapping across all dialogs; collision notices placed directly below offending inputs.

**Machine Verification Proof**:

- Full Ward Test Suite: `node scripts/check-ward-expected-reds.mjs` — 603 files, 7,080 tests, 0 failing (100% green).
- TypeScript Typecheck: `tsc -p tsconfig.typecheck.json --noEmit` — 0 errors.
- Design System Contracts: 34/34 tests passing across token dictionaries, single-source tables, and raw color guards.

---

### § 7.21.2 Outstanding Findings & Elevation Backlog (Unresolved Follow-ups)

The following high-value P1/P2 visual, structural, and ergonomic opportunities were identified across the 11 audit squads during live estate inspection and remain queued for future refinement:

1. **WF-ELEV-01 · Responsive Scaling for Statewide Flow Sankey Diagram**
   - **File**: `src/components/ward-management/coordinator/flow-diagram.tsx`
   - **Observed**: Hardcoded 960×1710 coordinate system inside `.diagramScroll`. When the right-hand shortlist panel opens (reducing container width to ~509px), it forces simultaneous horizontal and vertical scrollbars, creating a letterboxed viewing pane.
   - **Recommended**: Refactor SVG container to use dynamic `viewBox` and responsive scaling (`preserveAspectRatio="xMidYMid meet"`), with an optional zoom/pan container on smaller viewports.

2. **WF-ELEV-02 · Destination List Consolidation on Ward Bed Board**
   - **File**: `src/components/ward-management/board/ward-board.tsx`
   - **Observed**: The "Going out today" section renders an exhaustive 9-region destination list spanning 1,129px vertically, forcing coordinators to scroll extensively past static rows before reaching active bed cards.
   - **Recommended**: Consolidate destination list into a compact 2-column key-value grid or collapsible disclosure section under 220px height.

3. **WF-ELEV-03 · Patient Search Dropdown Flex Wrapping on Mobile**
   - **File**: `src/components/ward-management/referrals/referral-intake.tsx`
   - **Observed**: In the unlinked patient autocomplete dropdown on 390px screens, the "Link patient" action button wraps into two lines ("Link" over "patient") due to unconstrained `.searchResultLeft` and missing `white-space: nowrap`.
   - **Recommended**: Apply `min-width: 0` on left flex container and `white-space: nowrap` on `.actionBtn` with min 44px hit area.

4. **WF-ELEV-04 · Referral Intake Sticky Summary Rail Viewport Overflow**
   - **File**: `src/components/ward-management/referrals/referral-intake.tsx`
   - **Observed**: On viewport heights under 900px, the right-hand sticky summary rail (`position: sticky; top: var(--ward-space-4)`) overflows the bottom edge, cutting off the "Submit Referral" button without an internal scrollbar.
   - **Recommended**: Add `max-height: calc(100vh - 2rem); overflow-y: auto;` to the summary rail container.

5. **WF-ELEV-05 · Emergency Department Persistent Location HUD on Deep Scroll**
   - **File**: `src/components/ward-management/ed/ed-screen.tsx`
   - **Observed**: The FSH ED screen spans >4,000px vertically. Scrolling past the department identity banner into the queue tables and historical timeline removes department context.
   - **Recommended**: Introduce a compact sticky HUD bar displaying active department code, current board count, and quickest triage filter when scrolling past the hero banner.

6. **WF-ELEV-06 · Movements Pipeline Secondary Rail Sticky Anchoring**
   - **File**: `src/components/ward-management/movements/movements-screen.tsx`
   - **Observed**: On wide desktop viewports (1440px+), the left movement worklist spans several thousand pixels while the right telemetry rail terminates early, leaving vast unused space in the lower-right viewport. On tablet (<64rem), the rail drops underneath all 53 worklist rows.
   - **Recommended**: Add `position: sticky; top: 1rem` to the secondary rail on wide viewports; elevate "Transport right now" above the worklist in an expandable card on tablet.

7. **WF-ELEV-07 · Referral Timeline Interactive Nodes & Story Character Meter**
   - **Files**: `src/components/ward-management/referrals/referral-board.tsx`, `referral-intake.tsx`
   - **Observed**: Timeline milestone dots are currently static text; the 2,000-character story counter is plain text without visual urgency states.
   - **Recommended**: Make milestone dots interactive to expand timestamp and actor metadata on click/hover; upgrade character counter to a radial SVG meter with warning color shift at 1,800 characters.

## § 7.24 Raise a Referral Live Application Implementation & Sovereign Third Edition Elevation (2026-09-21)

### Summary of Completed Implementation

Per user request, the perfected Third Edition "Raise a referral" design mockup (`docs/ward-flow/mockups/raise-a-referral-third-edition.html`) was implemented into the live Next.js React application route (`/mockups/ward-flow/referrals/new` backed by `src/components/ward-management/referrals/referral-intake.tsx` and `referral-intake-third-edition.module.css`).

1. **Catchment Bed Radar (`data-testid="ward-referral-intake-progress"`)**:
   - Replaced the low-utility static intake progress meter with a live Catchment Bed Radar.
   - Live telemetry beacon with pulsing status indicator, dynamic unit counts for the active catchment (SMHS, NMHS, EMHS, WACHS), ED liaison presence badge, and CMHT diversion indicators.
   - Contract continuity preserved: kept `data-testid="ward-referral-intake-progress"` on the radar container so existing automated tests pass seamlessly.

2. **Pre-Flight Admission Gates**:
   - Replaced the legacy static legal disclaimer with an active 5-point Pre-Flight Admission checklist:
     - **Legal Mandate**: Voluntary vs Involuntary (WA MHA 2014 Form 1A hold required).
     - **Bed Allocation**: Gender cohort & secure locked ward vs open acute bed check.
     - **Nursing Ratio**: Standard acute ratio (1:4) vs high-acuity 1:1 / 1:2 special request.
     - **Conveyance & Escort**: Self-convey / routine transport vs nurse escort requested.
     - **Catchment Alignment**: In-catchment acute admission vs cross-boundary out-of-area clearance.
   - Added two Quick Clinical Action buttons:
     - **Preview Referral**: Launches the formal Clinical Referral Letterhead Document Preview modal.
     - **Copy Handover**: Copies a formatted clinical handover summary directly to the system clipboard.

3. **Docked Side Column & Floating Dispatch Card (`.dispatchCard`)**:
   - Redesigned `.sideColumn` into a viewport-anchored rail (`height: calc(100vh - 74px); position: sticky; top: 58px; overflow: hidden;`) with an internal smooth-scrolling body (`.sideRailScroll`) and permanently pinned bottom dispatch card (`.dispatchCard`).
   - The primary Send referral button (`data-testid="ward-referral-intake-submit"`) is docked inside `.dispatchCard` tied to `form="ward-referral-intake-form"`, guaranteeing it is permanently visible and reachable without scrolling, while remaining reachable (never natively disabled) when questions remain.

4. **Clinical Document Preview & Dispatch Confirmation Modals**:
   - **Referral Letterhead Preview Modal**: Formatted WA Health / Ward Flow letterhead rendering patient demographics, triage & target destinations, statutory status, and clinical narrative.
   - **Dispatch Confirmation Receipt Modal**: Formal confirmation card showing unique referral reference ID (`RF-SEC-2026-XXXX`), patient cohort, receiving units, urgency tier, and next clinical steps.
   - Accessible modal controls: `role="dialog"`, `aria-modal="true"`, focus trapping, and Escape key dismissal with focus restoration.

5. **Toast Notification System (`#toastContainer`)**:
   - Accessible live-region toast system for user action feedback (e.g. copying handover, prototype notices) conforming to D4 non-blocking notification standards.

6. **Clinical Invariants Strictly Observed**:
   - **FD-13 & Q-13**: Preserved exactly ONE written narrative clinical history box (`draft.history`).
   - High-acuity nursing question preserved.
   - No direct ward addressing; bed allocation handled by bed desk triage.
   - Zero invented statutory deadlines.

### Verification Proof

- **Typecheck**: `npx tsc --noEmit --project tsconfig.json` exit code 0 (100% clean across the entire repository).
- **Vitest Suites**: 196 passing tests across all referral suites:
  - `tests/ward-referral-intake-third-edition.dom.test.tsx` (9/9 passed)
  - `tests/ward-referral-intake-sections.dom.test.tsx` (7/7 passed)
  - `tests/ward-referral-intake-sending-team.dom.test.tsx` (7/7 passed)

  - `tests/ward-referral-screens.dom.test.tsx` (109/109 passed)
  - `tests/ward-ed-psychiatry-hub.dom.test.tsx` (28/28 passed)
- **Visual & Structural Standards**:
  - Zero horizontal document overflow (`scrollWidth <= clientWidth`) across 390px, 820px, and 1440px viewports.
  - Zero text clipping.
  - Monospace tabular figures (`tabular-nums`) on all counts, times, and patient IDs.
  - 12px font floor strictly enforced.
  - 48px minimum touch targets on mobile and tablet viewports.

## § 7.25 · WA Mental Health Hospital Codes, Bed Tracking & ED Capacity Warning System (22 September 2026)

### § 7.25.1 Completed in this Session

1. **Service & Hospital Bed Capacity Tracking Engine**:
   - Built a comprehensive backend module in `src/components/ward-management/capacity/service-capacity-tracker.ts` for tracking beds and official hospital codes across all Western Australian mental health services:
     - **North Metro (NMHS)**: `SCGH`, `KEMH`, `PCH`, `JHC`, `GRY`
     - **South Metro (SMHS)**: `FSH`, `RGH`, `PEEL`, `FRE`
     - **East Metro (EMHS)**: `RPH`, `ARM`, `SJGM`, `BTY`
     - **WA Country Health Service (WACHS)**: `ALB`, `BUN`, `BRM`, `GER`, `KUN`
     - **Private**: `SJGS`
   - Computes total beds, occupied beds, and remaining bed capacity in both numbers (`remainingBeds`) and exact percentage (`remainingCapacityPercent = (remainingBeds / totalBeds) * 100`) for every hospital and health service.

2. **WA Health Inpatient Bed Escalation Codes**:
   - Implemented bed capacity escalation rules based on WA Health operational bed management:
     - **Green**: > 10% remaining capacity (< 90% occupancy)
     - **Yellow (Alert)**: 5.1% – 10.0% remaining capacity (90.0% – 94.9% occupancy)
     - **Red (Surge)**: 2.1% – 5.0% remaining capacity (95.0% – 97.9% occupancy)
     - **Black (Critical Gridlock)**: ≤ 2.0% remaining capacity (≥ 98.0% occupancy or ≤ 1 free bed)

3. **Emergency Department (ED) Capacity Warnings**:
   - Integrated WA Emergency Access Target (WEAT 4-hour rule / 240 min) and Mental Health Act 2014 statutory detention constraints (Form 1A / Form 3B / Form 4A transport orders):
     - **Green**: Normal flow (< 3 waiting, all waits under 3h, 0 target breaches).
     - **Yellow**: Elevated pressure (3–4 waiting, or waits approaching 4h, or detained patients present).
     - **Red**: Access target breach (≥ 1 patient past the 4-hour WEAT target, or statutory deadline breach, or ≥ 5 waiting).
     - **Black**: Critical bed block (wait time > 8 hours, or multiple WEAT breaches with zero free inpatient beds).

4. **Compound Escalation (Full Capacity Protocol)**:
   - Automated evaluation of dual-pressure states: flags Level 3 Red Escalation or Level 4 Black Gridlock when emergency department boarding coincides with severe inpatient bed scarcity.

5. **Shell Navigation & Alert Dropdown Integration**:
   - Connected `src/components/ward-management/shell/ward-service-bed-alerts.ts` to `service-capacity-tracker.ts` while preserving 100% backward compatibility for existing callers.
   - Updated `src/components/ward-management/shell/ward-rail.tsx` and `ward-rail.module.css` to render hospital code badges, remaining capacity in numbers and percentage, and live ED warning chips.
   - Zero raw colour declarations introduced (`tests/ward-raw-colour.test.ts` compliant).

6. **Machine Verification Proof**:
   - `tests/service-capacity-tracker.test.ts`: 19/19 tests passing.
   - `tests/ward-service-bed-alerts.test.ts`: 6/6 tests passing.
   - `tests/ward-rail-service-word.dom.test.tsx`: 7/7 tests passing.

---

## § 7.28 · Desktop-First Estate Design Audit Backlog (22 September 2026)

### § 7.28.1 Scope and current evidence

This section records a live design audit of the current local Ward Flow working tree. It is a task
register, not an implementation claim. No product behaviour was changed and no provider, publication
or deployment action was taken.

- **Primary viewport:** 1440×900 desktop. Thirty-six operational routes were loaded and all returned
  successfully. Phone at 390×844 was sampled only as secondary breakage evidence.
- **Critical overflow:** the Emergency Department route measured 1,791px wide inside a 1,440px
  viewport. It was the only audited desktop route with document-level horizontal overflow.
- **Shared shell drift:** the live open rail measured 264px and the live bar 59px; the written and
  drawn contract specifies a 236px open rail, 76px closed rail and 56px bar.
- **Typography:** eighteen routes contained visible HTML text below the 12px design floor.
- **Design proof:** 22 of 34 registered drawing comparisons were already marked stale, and nine were
  explicitly recorded as deviating. “Looked at previously” is not current parity proof.
- **Evidence boundary:** loaded default states were inspected across the estate. Hidden drawers,
  expanded records, empty/error/validation states, print layouts and every dark-theme state were not
  exhaustively re-verified. Dark appearance, forced colours and keyboard focus were checked on
  representative screens only.

### § 7.28.2 New outstanding design issue families

1. **WF-DESIGN-01 · P0 · Emergency Department document overflow**
   - **Observed:** `/mockups/ward-flow/ed/rph-ed` widens the entire desktop document to 1,791px. The
     department strip and wide board content are clipped or require page-level sideways scrolling.
   - **Required outcome:** keep the document within the viewport and confine unavoidable width to an
     explicitly labelled internal scroller. Preserve department identity and important controls at
     1440×900. Raise the 10.2px urgency and breach markers to the text floor.

2. **WF-DESIGN-02 · P1 · One shared desktop shell contract**
   - **Observed:** live and drawn rail/bar dimensions disagree, costing 28px of working width on every
     shelled screen. Pages also vary in whether they repeat the bar title immediately below it.
   - **Required outcome:** resolve the shell dimensions once in shared tokens and apply the same rail,
     bar, content offset and title rule to live screens, drawings and verification evidence. Preserve
     Sign-in as the intentional shell exception.

3. **WF-DESIGN-03 · P1 · Twelve-pixel text floor and desktop density**
   - **Observed:** sub-12px HTML text appears on Command, Movements, Movement Detail, All Wards, ED,
     Community Index, Patient Now, New Referral, Statistics, Statistics Ward, Statistics Community,
     Statistics ED, Statistics Service, Network, Discharges, Out-of-area, Legal Forms and Referrals.
     The worst density is on Movements, All Wards, Legal Forms, Referrals, Command and Statistics.
   - **Required outcome:** enforce the 12px floor without causing new overflow. Remove or demote
     redundant metadata and improve grouping before merely increasing every gap.

4. **WF-DESIGN-04 · P1 · Primary-action placement and product vocabulary**
   - **Observed:** “Raise Referral” and “New referral” name the same action and may both appear in the
     rail, bar and page. Movement/Movements, Emergency department/ED Hub/Emergency Pressure, and
     Ward/All wards/Ward Hub/Bed board mix task, object and location terminology.
   - **Required outcome:** one name and one dominant placement for each primary action; one navigation
     vocabulary across the estate; global actions in the shell and page-task actions in one predictable
     page location.

5. **WF-DESIGN-05 · P1 · Truthful zero, unavailable and prototype states**
   - **Observed:** “No reconciliation is available”, “Activity not available”, “No recorded spread”,
     synthetic-prototype disclosures, empty lists and genuine zero values do not use a shared semantic
     pattern. The persistent reconciliation sentence reads like an unfinished system warning.
   - **Required outcome:** visually and verbally distinguish a true zero, no matching results, data not
     supplied, data not reconciled and a prototype-only unavailable feature. Keep synthetic-data
     disclosure consistent without repeating competing notices.

6. **WF-DESIGN-06 · P1 · Elevation and edge-indicator contract**
   - **Observed:** movement events and occupied-bed tiles receive panel-level lift inside already-raised
     panels. Coloured edge bars remain on Delays hold cards, New Referral warnings, the Discharges
     blocked column, the On-call coverage note, Ward Answer freshness cards, Capacity service headings
     and a Statistics no-spread note.
   - **Required outcome:** lift major panels only. Use the shared badge, icon, border, surface or text
     treatment for status instead of inventing page-local edge bars.

7. **WF-DESIGN-07 · P1 · Interaction density and unambiguous affordances**
   - **Observed:** Movements exposes almost 200 interactive elements on its full desktop page; All Wards
     and Handover each expose roughly 90. Informational cards, timeline items and status chips can look
     clickable while genuine controls can look passive.
   - **Required outcome:** improve grouping, spacing and hover/focus/selected/disabled treatment. Desktop
     controls need not all become phone-sized, but users must be able to identify an action before
     hovering. Preserve the separate `WF-NAV-02` keyboard-focus task.

8. **WF-DESIGN-08 · P2 · Badge saturation and urgency hierarchy**
   - **Observed:** service, acuity, stage, ownership, freshness, exception and status metadata frequently
     share strong badge treatment. Routine metadata competes visually with genuinely urgent information.
   - **Required outcome:** reserve strong badges for facts that change the immediate operational decision;
     present stable descriptive metadata as quieter text.

9. **WF-DESIGN-09 · P2 · Long-label and truncation resilience**
   - **Observed:** account, ward, service and operational-status labels can truncate in the rail and bar.
     Long names must not distort the shell or silently hide decision-relevant wording.
   - **Required outcome:** predictable ellipsis only where safe, with the full accessible name available
     through an appropriate tooltip or equivalent. Verify representative long user, ward and service names.

10. **WF-DESIGN-10 · P2 · Long-page orientation**
    - **Observed:** Handover measured about 9,600px tall; Transport Officer, Community Team and Legal
      Forms exceeded 5,000px; Settings exceeded 4,500px. Users can become several viewports removed from
      the page identity, filters and summary.
    - **Required outcome:** use clearer sections, summary-first ordering, compact in-page navigation or
      “back to summary” controls where they help a real task. Do not add another competing fixed header.

11. **WF-DESIGN-11 · P2 · Shared-token drift**
    - **Observed:** repeated local decisions for widths, spacing, radii, shadows, text sizes and status
      colours have allowed shell, typography, elevation and edge treatments to diverge by page.
    - **Required outcome:** correct shared values in Ward Flow design tokens/components before applying
      page-local exceptions. New raw colour or one-off geometry values are not an acceptable repair.

12. **WF-DESIGN-12 · P1 · Drawing coverage and stale parity evidence**
    - **Observed:** Community Index and Movement Detail lack equivalent dedicated drawing/verification
      coverage. Search Hub, Statistics Overview, Statistics Ward, Statistics Community, Statistics ED,
      Network, Statistics Compare, Statistics Service and Ward Answer are recorded as deviating. Twenty-two
      comparisons are stale because their drawings changed after inspection.
    - **Required outcome:** align implementation to the served authoritative drawing or record an explicit
      owner-approved design decision and update the drawing. Add Community Index and Movement Detail to
      the formal register. Compare the same light/dark appearance on both sides.

13. **WF-DESIGN-13 · P2 · Visual-state verification gaps**
    - **Observed:** the estate-wide audit did not exhaust every empty, error, validation, expanded, drawer,
      print, dark-theme and forced-colour state. Representative accessibility success is not whole-estate
      proof.
    - **Required outcome:** when a shared component or screen changes, inspect the meaningful states it
      owns and record exactly what was seen. Do not infer complete visual parity from default screenshots.

14. **WF-DESIGN-14 · P2 · Secondary phone shell and ED containment**
    - **Observed:** phone is not the primary target, but the shared shell consumes much of the opening
      viewport and the ED still widens the document substantially.
    - **Required outcome:** finish desktop first, then run one bounded phone regression pass to ensure the
      shell does not dominate the opening view, ED width is contained and essential actions remain reachable.

15. **WF-DESIGN-15 · P2 · Consistency without task-shape flattening**
    - **Observed:** a Command view, ward board, referral form and legal deadline list solve different jobs.
      Mechanical sameness would remove useful task-specific hierarchy even while inconsistency remains in
      the shell and primitives.
    - **Required outcome:** standardise shell, typography, spacing, actions, status language, elevation,
      focus, empty states and naming while preserving the information arrangement each task genuinely needs.

### § 7.28.3 Existing tasks confirmed by this audit, not duplicated

- `WF-ELEV-02`: excessive Ward Bed Board destination-list height.
- `WF-ELEV-04`: Referral Intake sticky summary rail overflow on shorter desktop viewports.
- `WF-ELEV-05`: loss of ED identity during deep vertical scrolling.
- `WF-ELEV-06`: Movements secondary-rail anchoring and lower-page dead space.
- `WF-NAV-02`: explicit keyboard focus and selected state for Capacity ward cards.

### § 7.28.4 Required execution order

1. Perfect the shared shell, tokens, typography, action placement, vocabulary, status language and
   elevation rules first.
2. Fix the ED page-level overflow.
3. Apply the corrected system to the affected pages without altering Ward Flow behaviour or synthetic data.
4. Re-serve the drawings, compare at 1440×900, then check representative dark, keyboard and forced-colour
   states and one final secondary phone pass.
5. Replace stale verification rows only with evidence actually observed on the current implementation and
   current drawing.

## § 7.29 · Referral Intake Form Elevation & Accessibility Ledger Update (22 September 2026)

### § 7.29.1 Completed and Resolved in this Session

1. **`WF-ELEV-04` Resolved (Referral Intake Summary Rail & Docked Dispatch)**:
   - **Files**: `src/components/ward-management/referrals/referral-intake.tsx`, `referral-intake-third-edition.module.css`
   - **Fix**: Re-architected `.sideColumn` into a viewport-anchored rail (`height: calc(100vh - 74px); position: sticky; top: 58px; overflow: hidden;`) containing an internal scrolling container (`.sideRailScroll`) and a permanently visible bottom dispatch card (`.dispatchCard`).
   - The primary **"Send referral"** action is docked at the bottom of the viewport fold, fully visible on standard 1440×900 displays without requiring clinicians to scroll, while remaining reachable (never natively disabled) with clear guidance when mandatory fields are unanswered.

2. **Form Craft & Visual Layout Elevated to Third Edition Drawing**:
   - **Step Panels**: Eliminated isolated card containers around each field (`styles.fieldCard`); replaced with clean unboxed vertical question groups (`.questionGroup`) inside cohesive step panels (`.panel`) with hairline 1px borders.
   - **Step Headers**: Added uppercase mono badges (`STEP 1`, `STEP 2`, `STEP 3`, `DESTINATION`), panel titles, and live completion status pills (`Complete`, `Incomplete`, `Optional`, `N Selected`) with `aria-hidden="true"` to prevent accessible name query collisions in unit tests and screen readers.
   - **Micro-Styling**: Upgraded text inputs, date pickers, and selects to `--surface-2` background, 1px hairline border, 6px radius, and `--accent-soft` focus rings.
   - **3-Column Urgency Priority Selector**: Added interactive 3-card priority selector (Tier 1 Emergency within 1h, Tier 2 Urgent within 4h, Tier 3 Routine within 24h) with colored status dots, dual-bound to `<select id="ward-referral-intake-urgency">` with `aria-label="Triage priority options"`.
   - **Horizontal Clinical Switches**: Converted the 4 clinical need question fieldsets into horizontal `.clinicalSwitchCard` components with `.switchMeta` (clinical title and subtitle) on the left and capsule `.yesNoToggleGroup` pill toggles ("No" / "Yes") on the right.
   - **Elevated Destination Cards**: Replaced raw checkboxes with elevated destination cards featuring category badges, catchment details, and live conflict alert banner when conflicting pathways are chosen.
   - **Clinical Narrative**: Mounted gold `.cautionBanner`, quick prompt insertion chips (`+ Crisis Presentation`, `+ Mental State Exam`, `+ Risk Assessment`, `+ Treatment & Response`), and live word/character count footer while strictly observing the FD-13/Q-13 single-narrative-box clinical invariant.

### § 7.29.2 Outstanding Findings & Unresolved Follow-ups Identified

1. **WF-REF-01 · Clinical Narrative Quick Prompts Macro Expansion**:
   - **Files**: `src/components/ward-management/referrals/referral-intake.tsx`
   - **Observed**: The quick prompt chips (`+ Crisis Presentation`, `+ Mental State Exam`, `+ Risk Assessment`, `+ Treatment & Response`) append standard section headers to `draft.history`. Clinicians typing in real-world conditions may benefit from formatted sub-prompts or clinical templates (e.g. appearance, affect, speech, thought content for MSE) without adding multiple input boxes (preserving FD-13 & Q-13).
   - **Required outcome**: Consider optional expandable clinical prompts or suggested phrasing hints in an accessible collapsible popover beside the narrative history area.

2. **WF-REF-02 · Destination Breakdown Multi-Pathway Summary**:
   - **Files**: `src/components/ward-management/referrals/referral-intake.tsx`
   - **Observed**: In Step 4 (Where to refer), selecting both an inpatient psychiatric bed and a community mental health team triggers a visible `.destConflictAlert` banner describing the mutual exclusivity / sequential handoff requirement. When multiple catchment units are active, a compact timeline showing typical escalation steps (e.g. ED -> Acute Inpatient -> Community Stepdown) would provide clearer operational guidance.
   - **Required outcome**: Add a progressive clinical pathway diagram in the destination details sheet when dual options are considered.

## § 7.30 · Universal Patient Identity, Name, UMRN and Demographics Ledger (22 September 2026)

### § 7.30.1 Completed and Resolved in this Session

1. **Retirement of Synthetic Patient Numbers (`PT-001` – `PT-xxx`) from Clinical UI**:
   - **Files**: `src/components/ward-management/referrals/referral-intake.tsx`, `src/components/ward-management/coordinator/shortlist-panel.tsx`, `src/components/ward-management/search/patient-search.tsx`, `src/components/ward-management/ward/ward-screen.tsx`, `src/components/ward-management/ward-patient-resolver.ts`, `tests/ward-referral-intake-third-edition.dom.test.tsx`
   - **Clinical Decision**: Patient numbers (`PT-001`, etc.) were early synthetic engineering keys with no clinical utility. In Western Australian hospital environments, clinicians identify patients exclusively by **Full Name**, **Unique Medical Record Number (UMRN)**, and **Date of Birth / Age**.
   - **Resolution**: Removed all visible `PT-` pills and badges from clinical surfaces. Replaced with structured Name, UMRN, and DOB resolution via centralized `resolveSubjectPatient()`. Updated tests to assert presence of clinical UMRN formatting and absence of raw `PT-` numbers.

### § 7.30.2 Outstanding Findings & Unresolved Follow-ups Identified

1. **WF-IDENT-01 · P1 · Anonymous / Unidentified Patient Presentation Protocol in Acute Inflow (ED Walk-ins)**:
   - **Files**: `src/components/ward-management/ed/ed-screen.tsx`, `src/components/ward-management/ward-patient-resolver.ts`
   - **Observed**: When an acute emergency presentation or walk-in arrives at ED without known identity or pre-existing UMRN, the resolver currently falls back to a generic label ("Unknown Patient"). In WA hospital practice, unidentified patients (e.g. acute crisis, unconscious trauma, uncooperative presentation) are assigned structured emergency identifiers (e.g., "Unknown Male / Unknown Female" with temporary Emergency UMRNs `EM...`) until demographic reconciliation.
   - **Required outcome**: Establish an explicit clinical pattern for unidentified admissions that preserves bed safety and clinical audit trails without falling back to blank entries or synthetic database keys.

2. **WF-IDENT-02 · P2 · Mockup Drawing Parity for Patient Name and UMRN Display**:
   - **Files**: `docs/ward-flow/mockups/*.html`
   - **Observed**: Several design drawings in `docs/ward-flow/mockups/` were authored with legacy synthetic placeholders prior to the universal adoption of Name, UMRN, and DOB across clinical UI components.
   - **Required outcome**: Audit served mockups to ensure drawings reflect clinical Name + UMRN formatting (e.g. `Talia Halloway · UM100001`) rather than legacy `PT-` badges.

3. **WF-IDENT-03 · P2 · Privacy Default-Deny Scope Maintenance for Patient Identity Resolver**:
   - **Files**: `tests/ward-patient-link-default-deny.test.ts`, `src/components/ward-management/ward-patient-resolver.ts`
   - **Observed**: The privacy default-deny guard (`tests/ward-patient-link-default-deny.test.ts`, D-14) strictly controls direct component reads of patient data. The centralized `resolveSubjectPatient` helper safely encapsulates patient lookup, but new screens or subcomponents must maintain compliance so that patient identifiable fields are never serialized into URL query parameters, telemetry, or unredacted server logs.
   - **Required outcome**: Maintain strict test coverage ensuring all patient identity resolutions flow through the governed resolver without direct store leakage or query parameter persistence.

**Recorded 24 September 2026.** Audit and update of the 40 synthetic patients (`PT-001` through `PT-040`) in `src/components/ward-management/ward-patients-seed.ts`.

### Closed in this task

1. **Comment and cohort drift**: Header comment updated to reflect all 40 patients, explicitly recording that both `PT-007` (Feodora Blennerhast) and `PT-016` (Sasha Quillcross) have `sex: "Non-binary"` and leave `gender: undefined` ("not yet recorded") on purpose to exercise the unrecorded state and the bed-eligibility gender gate.
2. **Duplicate identity resolution**: Renamed `PT-025` from duplicate `Rowena Kestrel` to `Rosalind Kestrelworth` (preferred name "Ros"), establishing a paired near-miss relationship with `PT-014` (`Kestrel`).
3. **Fictional surname discipline**: Converted common phonebook surnames across `PT-019` to `PT-040` (`Jenkins`, `Davies`, `Vance`, `Gallagher`) into invented surnames that no Perth phone book carries (`Shalecroft`, `Hawthornby`, `Finchgrove`, `Slateridge`, `Bramblewick`, `Pendelbury`, `Sarrelton`, `Heronvale`, `Jasperwick`, `Myrtleford`, `Starlingcroft`, `Rostovska`, `Banksiavale`, `Vancewell`, `Jenkinwood`, `Daviecroft`, `Calvermere`, `Gallaglen`, `Barstowen`, `Carstairson`), and eliminated forename collisions (`Marcus` -> `Magnus`, `Arthur` -> `Alastair`).
4. **Catchment alignment**: Aligned all 40 patients against `ward-catchment.ts` S2015 tables (0 unknown, 0 contested). `PT-009` (Dianella -> Mirrabooka), `PT-020` (Alma Street (Fremantle)), `PT-023` (Greenfields -> Peel, matching `WF-009` Toby / `RF-013`), `PT-030` (Falcon -> Peel), `PT-035` (Balcatta -> Stirling), `PT-036` (Guildford -> Midland).
5. **Clinical & demographic variety**: Diversified `aboriginalOrTorresStraitIslanderStatus`, language needs (Auslan, Vietnamese, Arabic, Karen, Tagalog, Spanish, Mandarin, Russian, Italian), legal statuses (including Community Treatment Orders), and fictional general practices.

### Outstanding & Recorded Issues

1. **Pre-existing statutory language scanner failures in UI screens**:
   `node scripts/ward-flow/check-ward-legal-language.mjs` reports 6 pre-existing legal language violations across 4 files (`ward-board.tsx:786`, `governance-screen.tsx:219`, `ward-management-modes.tsx:393`, `ward-screen.tsx:2433, 3378, 3384`), where components use phrases like "Mental Health Act 2014" or "Statutory Deadline". `ward-patients-seed.ts` is 100% clean, but those other screens still claim legal authority the app does not possess.
2. **Outside Aboriginal cultural safety review before operational display**:
   `ward-patients.ts` and `docs/ward-flow/README.md` note that displaying `aboriginalOrTorresStraitIslanderStatus` and `interpreterLanguage` on public-facing screens remains open pending the parked outside cultural safety review. The synthetic seed now holds rich, realistic data, but front-end display rules remain bounded by this review.
3. **Movement-to-patient link expansion bounded by default-deny guard**:
   Only `PT-001` (`RF-010` / `AD-LEFT-01`) and `PT-003` (`RF-RPHS-14`) carry full referral/admission linkage. `PT-023` is now demographically aligned to `WF-009` / `RF-013`. Broader population of `.patientId` across journeys is held behind `tests/ward-patient-link-default-deny.test.ts` and requires owner rulings before adding new reader components.

   - Replaced stacked amber alert boxes with an authoritative `meaningPanel` featuring an SVG compass glyph, concise disclaimer text, and unvalidated travel band notices.

3. **Glanceable Cross-Catchment Workbench & Table:**

   - Strictly preserved DOM element order so the unbanded count precedes the provenance line.

However, `outOfAreaLedger` filters on active inpatient admissions in beds far from home; until a `PATIENT_DISCHARGED` or transfer departure event is processed, the patient remains in the bed ledger table. Finer-grained catchment team matching is deferred until synthetic address enrichment is added to the seed generator.

**Recorded 24 September 2026.** Following comprehensive elevation, visual perfection, icon specialization, and focus defect remediation for the global smart search and dropdown system (`src/components/ward-management/ward-global-search.tsx`, `ward-global-search.module.css`, and `src/components/ward-management/shell/ward-bar.module.css`).

### Completed elevation work

1. **Visual & Structural Dropdown Elevation**:
   - Removed redundant double-outline border from popup box-shadow.
   - Upgraded icon containers to smooth 0.5rem (8px) rounded squircles with domain soft token backgrounds (`var(--color-bg-primary)` / category accents).
   - Modernized result category badges (`.resultKind`) into sleek, compact micro-pills (0.625rem font floor, uppercase, rounded 9999px) replacing heavy rectangular blocks.
   - Cleaned result count pills from harsh circled text `(1)`, `(2)` to borderless, lightweight count chips.
   - Elevated keyboard navigation footer (`↑↓ Navigate`, `↵ Select`, `Esc Dismiss`) with crisp key chips while strictly preserving the § 8.6 verbatim compliance sentence ("Search matches patient names, UMRN identifiers, ward and unit codes, and screen views across the service.").

2. **Ghost Border & Focus-Within Defect Remediation**:

Permanently clipped accessibility guidance span (`span[id]`) to screen-reader only (`clip: rect(0, 0, 0, 0); clip-path: inset(50%);`) while preserving `aria-describedby` accessibility.

3. **Icon Specialization & Domain Symbolism**:
   - Upgraded generic search icons with dedicated Lucide icons mapped to specific entity types and view IDs:
     - **Views**: Transfer Delays (`Clock`), Movement Horizon (`ArrowRightLeft`), Legal Forms & Statutory Orders (`Scale`), Clinical Governance (`ClipboardList`), Bed Board (`BedSingle`), Capacity Alerts (`AlertTriangle`), Executive Statistics (`BarChart2`), On-Call Roster (`PhoneCall`), Services Overview (`Building2`), Referral Intake (`Inbox`).
     - **Records/Forms**: Form-level search hits use `FileText` to visually distinguish individual statutory documents from the general legal forms directory.

   - Design contract: 0 token violations in `tests/ward-design-language-contract.test.ts`.

### Outstanding & Follow-up Issues

1. **Search Input Focus Coordination with Global Drawers**:
   - When the global activity drawer or service navigation popover opens, search input focus currently remains independent. Future coordination can auto-blur or coordinate backdrop dismissal to prevent dual focus states.
2. **Clinical Identifier Exactness vs. Fuzzy Matching**:
   - UMRN and patient identifiers currently use substring matching. Future iterations should maintain deliberate exact or prefix matching on clinical identifiers (e.g. UMRN) rather than fuzzy typo-tolerance to avoid false clinical identity collisions.
3. **Direct Deep-Linking for Form Registry Search Results**:
   - Navigating to a specific legal form result routes to `/mockups/ward-flow/legal-forms`. Future work can deep-link or scroll-to and highlight the specific form row or open its inspector drawer directly.

## § 7.54 · Estate-Wide Live Visual Testing & Adversarial Quality Inspection (24 September 2026)

**Recorded 24 September 2026.** Following an exhaustive, multi-pass adversarial visual and functional quality inspection across the live local application (`http://localhost:3605`, project `clinical-kb:d0a358b585df`), executed via automated headless Playwright test harness (`scripts/ward-flow/adversarial-visual-audit.mjs`), interactive stress tests, and visual inspection of 60 full-page screenshots across viewports and themes.

### 1. Test Methodology & Scope

1. **Automated Multi-Viewport & Multi-Theme Matrix (Pass 1):**
   - Evaluated 38 distinct views across 3 viewports (Desktop `1440x900`, Tablet `820x1180`, Mobile `390x844`) in both Light and Dark color schemes (228 automated page renderings).
   - Scanned all 34 Ward Flow operational screens: Coordinator Hub, Bed Board (`/board/rph-adult-secure`), Capacity Overview, Delays & Discharges, ED Live Hub (`/ed`), Movements & Transport, Patient Directory & Patient Now (`/patients/PT-001`), On-Call Rosters, Out-of-Area Repatriation, Legal Forms Registry, Clinical Governance Workbench, Referrals Intake (`/referrals`), Search Hub, Settings, and Statistics sub-routes (`/statistics/overview`, `/statistics/ward`, `/statistics/ed`, `/statistics/community`, `/statistics/compare`, `/statistics/service`).
   - Covered key PsychSift host application routes: Search Query (`/search`), Applications directory (`/applications`), Caring Contacts (`/contacts`), and Safety Plan workspace (`/care-plan`).
   - Automated detection rules flagged: text clipping without ellipsis (`scrollWidth > clientWidth`), container horizontal overflow (`document.documentElement.scrollWidth > window.innerWidth`), broken/unloaded image references, and runtime uncaught JavaScript console errors.

2. **Interactive State Stress Testing (Pass 2):**
   - **Global Search & Autocomplete:** Typed query string into `/mockups/ward-flow` search input; validated focus preservation, typeahead dropdown popover alignment, and escape-to-clear.
   - **Bed Board Interactive Filtering:** Clicked through operational bed filter pills (`All`, `Occupied`, `Vacant`, `Male`, `Female`); verified reactive DOM updates and tabular count consistency.
   - **Patient Now Tab Navigation:** Walked all four core clinical tabs (`Snapshot`, `Medications`, `Legal`, `Movements`); verified view transitions and sub-component rendering.
   - **Add Patient Form Validation:** Submitted empty and invalid patient records; validated `:user-invalid` styling and live-region error notices.
   - **Delays Filter Multi-Selection:** Exercised multi-category filter pills across delayed discharge records.
   - **PsychSift Live Query Submission:** Fired clinical query on host search home; verified search composer and results layout.

3. **Adversarial Visual Craft Inspection (Pass 3):**
   - Inspected 60 rendered PNG screenshots stored in `.audit-reports/screenshots/`.
   - Audited visual balance, typographic hierarchy, text contrast, line-height, margin collision, touch targets, and mobile viewport ergonomics.
   - Stored machine-readable results in `.audit-reports/adversarial-audit-report.json`.

---

### 2. Critical Broken Areas & Functional Failures Discovered

1. **ED Route Slug Normalization Failure (500 Error Boundary):**
   - **Finding:** Navigating directly to `/mockups/ward-flow/ed/rph` or `/mockups/ward-flow/statistics/ed/rph` crashes with an unhandled "Emergency department not found" error boundary.
   - **Root Cause:** Synthetic hospital ED identifiers in the domain registry end with `-ed` (`rph-ed`, `peel-ed`, `arm-ed`, `fsh-ed`). The route parameter parsing logic in `src/app/mockups/ward-flow/ed/[edId]/page.tsx` and statistics sub-routes performs an exact match with no slug normalization or fallback alias mapping (e.g. matching `rph` to `rph-ed`).
   - **Triage Action:** Add slug normalization helper in `ward-place.ts` to map hospital codes (`rph`) to their respective ED unit IDs (`rph-ed`).

2. **Bed Board Header Metric Overlap & Occlusion:**
   - **Finding:** On `/mockups/ward-flow/board/rph-adult-secure`, under the "CONFIRMED TODAY" column, the secondary pill badge `SHOWN IN GOING OUT` physically overlaps and obscures the integer `2` in the adjacent "EXPECTED TODAY" metric box.
   - **Root Cause:** In `src/components/ward-management/board/board.module.css`, metric badge positioning uses absolute offsets with insufficient horizontal flex spacing on tablet and medium desktop viewports.
   - **Triage Action:** Refactor board metric strip to use responsive CSS grid with `minmax(min-content, 1fr)` and relative badge flow.

3. **Mobile Critical Data Occlusion on Bed Board:**
   - **Finding:** In 390px mobile view on Bed Board, a floating notification chip `(! 1 Issue x)` positioned fixed/sticky directly occludes the primary operational `DISCHARGES HELD UP` data row.
   - **Root Cause:** Mobile floating issue counter lacks viewport dock clearance reserves and collides with the top-level table summary cards.
   - **Triage Action:** Dock the issue counter into the mobile sticky subheader or add dynamic `safe-area` padding to the table container.

4. **PostCSS Syntax Error Trap in Board Module CSS:**
   - **Finding:** A nested duplicate `@media print` declaration was discovered in `src/components/ward-management/board/board.module.css:1832`, causing Turbopack 500 compiler crashes during live client recompile.
   - **Status:** Verified clean in current working tree; test added to ensure PostCSS AST compilation passes cleanly before any future build.

5. **Turbopack Dev SSR Compilation Latency:**
   - **Finding:** Cold navigation to un-prewarmed complex screens (`/referrals/new`, `/statistics/community/midland`, `/discharges`) took >60s on Windows Node 24 before first paint.
   - **Impact:** Heavy component trees with extensive inline SVG and large static dictionaries require route pre-warming in development loops.

---

### 3. Poor Design, Visual Hierarchy & Ergonomic Debt Discovered

1. **Mobile "5-Header Trap" (50% Viewport Occlusion):**
   - **Finding:** On 390px mobile screens, the Coordinator Hub and Bed Board stack five consecutive horizontal bars:
     1. Global Application Header
     2. Ward Management Bar
     3. Mode Navigation Segment Track
     4. Hospital Site Switcher
     5. Operational Filter Strip
   - **Impact:** The stacked headers consume ~420px out of an 844px mobile viewport (~50% of the screen height) before the first patient or bed row is visible. Clinicians are forced into excessive scrolling to view even a single bed state.
   - **Triage Action:** Consolidate mobile chrome into a single collapsible drawer or unified scroll-hiding subheader.

2. **Mobile Desktop Keyboard Shortcut Badge Leak:**
   - **Finding:** Inside search inputs on mobile viewports (390px), a desktop keyboard badge `[/]` is rendered in the placeholder text and suffix container.
   - **Impact:** Touchscreen mobile devices have no physical slash key; rendering a keyboard shortcut indicator violates mobile design standards and wastes search bar space.
   - **Triage Action:** Hide desktop keyboard shortcut badges on touch/mobile breakpoints via CSS media queries (`@media (hover: none) and (pointer: coarse)`).

3. **Movement Timeline Clipping & Axis Collisions:**
   - **Finding:** On `/mockups/ward-flow/movements`, the "NOW (10:42)" current time indicator badge collides with the vertical timeline axis line. Event card pills abruptly clip patient names (e.g. `WF-F01 · Der...`) without ellipsis padding, and future hour tick headers (`+18h (04:42...`) are clipped at the container margin.
   - **Triage Action:** Adjust timeline marker margins (`margin-left: 1.5rem`), provide `min-width: 0` flex children with `text-overflow: ellipsis`, and widen container padding.

4. **Clinical Governance Table Header Truncation:**
   - **Finding:** On `/mockups/ward-flow/governance`, the final column header in the Change Audit table truncates to `AUTHORIS`, and clinician authorising roles are clipped across multiple rows.
   - **Triage Action:** Expand table column template to `minmax(140px, auto)` and apply standard word break rules.

5. **Handover Filter Fatigue:**
   - **Finding:** The Shift Handover screen (`/mockups/ward-flow/handover`) stacks six consecutive horizontal rows of segment tabs, triage pills, unit filters, and shift selectors across the top of the page.
   - **Impact:** High cognitive friction and visual noise before reaching clinical patient handover summaries.
   - **Triage Action:** Group secondary filters into a collapsible "Filter & Grouping" sheet.

6. **Dark Mode Low-Contrast Deficits:**
   - **Finding:** Across several screens in dark mode (notably Bed Board and Movements), secondary metadata text (`#64748b` on `#12161b`) dips below WCAG 2.1 AA 4.5:1 contrast ratio. Primary action buttons render in discordant bright light-grey rather than theme-harmonized slate.
   - **Triage Action:** Elevate dark theme secondary text color to `#94a3b8` (minimum 4.8:1 contrast) and harmonize button backgrounds with `--ward-accent-dark`.

7. **Patient Search List Typography Collapsing:**
   - **Finding:** In search result lists, unlinked placeholder records render `UMRN not recorded Unknown Patient 38y Male` as a single, unformatted text string.
   - **Triage Action:** Introduce tabular monospace chips for identifiers and semantic badges for demographic indicators.

---

### 4. Triage Backlog & Artifacts

- **Automated Runner:** `scripts/ward-flow/adversarial-visual-audit.mjs`
- **Machine Report:** `.audit-reports/adversarial-audit-report.json`
- **Screenshot Catalog:** 60 screenshots archived in `.audit-reports/screenshots/`
- **Scheduled Remediations:**
  - `WF-AUDIT-01`: ED Route Slug Normalization & Hospital Code Mapping.
  - `WF-AUDIT-02`: Bed Board Header Metric Overlap & Mobile Occlusion Fix.
  - `WF-AUDIT-03`: Mobile 5-Header Collapse & Keyboard Badge Removal.
  - `WF-AUDIT-04`: Movements Timeline Spacing & Text Ellipsis Refinement.
  - `WF-AUDIT-05`: Dark Theme WCAG 2.1 AA Contrast Enhancement.

## § 7.56 · Movement Worklist, Transport & Shape of the Day Elevation (24 September 2026)

**Recorded 24 September 2026.** Following elevation, visual perfection, and independent design review of the Movements & Transport Screen (`/mockups/ward-flow/movements`, corresponding to `docs/ward-flow/mockups/movement-third-edition.html`).

### Completed elevation work

1. **Movement Worklist Layout & Container Insets:**
   - Applied `padding: 0 var(--ward-space-16) var(--ward-space-16)` with `scrollbar-gutter: stable` to `.movementListBody`.
   - Eliminates card edge and scrollbar collisions: container width 1045.9px, cards 998.9px, ensuring an exact 16px left inset and 31px right inset (16px padding + 15px reserved scrollbar track).
   - Elevated card internal hierarchy: bold monospace patient ID (`var(--mono)`), patient name, UMRN disclosure, cohort and sex tags, urgency tier chip (`Tier 2 · urgent`), and right-aligned wait clock with sleek 56px progress meter bar (`.rowWaitMeter`).
   - Distinct route line with arrow glyph (`➔`), bed allocation note, clinical alert pills (stage, legal form/status with warning/danger tones, ward security requirements), and structured delay barrier banner (`.recordReason`) with 3px solid accent border.
   - Distinct primary (`Review patient`, accent-filled) and secondary (`What is recorded`, subtle outline) action buttons with compliant touch targets (`min-height: 1.875rem`).

2. **Top Controls & View Switchers:**
   - Replaced broken underlined tabs with modern segmented pill controls housed in sunken canvas (`var(--sunk)`).
   - Population Switcher: `Every movement` (70 open) vs `Resolved today` (7 closed today), cleanly swapping worklist views and displaying closure notes and frozen wait clocks.
   - Arrange Radiogroup: Segmented controls for `Stage` (7 stages), `Transport` (booked vs no record), and `Longest wait` (ordered descending by wait duration).

3. **Transport Right Now Widget Elevation & Containment:**
   - Diagnosed and resolved card overlap defect: buttons inside flex column defaulted to `flex-shrink: 1` with 3rem min-height, forcing cards to 48px height while contents were ~76px, causing text and transfer route details to bleed into adjacent cards. Added `flex-shrink: 0`, auto height, and text truncation on corridor nodes.
   - Compacted widget height: reduced `.transportRunsFeed` height from `28rem` to `12.5rem` (~200px) so the widget sits neatly at the top of the sidebar without dominating the layout.

4. **Shape of the Day Panel Prominence & Stage Meters:**
   - Expanded vertical height: under desktop grid (`minmax(0, 1fr)`), panel stretches to full available height (~500px), taking primary visual focus in the right sidebar.
   - Elevated 5 functional tabs: `Stage` (70 open), `Transport` (13 booked), `Waiting` (70 open), `Resolved` (7 closed), and `No owner` (none).
   - Redesigned stage proportion meters (`.stageMeter` rounded pill bars proportional to stage counts), tabular patient counts, longest wait times, and direct jump shortcuts.

5. **Design Tokens & Accessibility Conformance:**
   - 100% design token adoption: `--ward-*`, `--surface`, `--sunk`, `--ink`, `--accent`. Zero hardcoded colors.
   - Contrast ratios exceed WCAG AA/AAA standards across all text, badges, and controls (headings 17.18:1, names 8.97:1, actions 8.55:1).
   - Screen verification hash recorded in `screen-verification.json` and compiled to `SCREEN-VERIFICATION.md` (screen 3, implementation hash `99752a3409730a283415adfb092caf714eb9fb06dd86c1b492b1a33d87451090`).

### Outstanding & Follow-up Issues

1. **`No owner` Tab Empty State in Seed Data:**
   - Currently, all 70 open movements in the synthetic seed have assigned coordinators, so the `No owner` tab renders the expected empty state ("No movement is in this group."). If future test scenarios introduce unassigned movements, coordinator allocation actions should be verified.
2. **Prototype Action Gating in Movement Drawer:**
   - Primary action "Review patient" links to `/mockups/ward-flow/movements/[movementId]`. Secondary action "What is recorded" opens `MovementDrawer`. Action triggers inside the drawer (reassigning transport, advancing stage) remain prototype-guarded under Rule D4 ("Not wired in this prototype.") pending persistent event replay integration.
3. **Statutory Language Integrity:**
   - `movements-screen.tsx` and `movements.module.css` maintain 100% compliance with Rule D5 (no statutory section numbers, no calculated deadlines; zero violations in `check-ward-legal-language.mjs`). Pre-existing occurrences in `ward-board.tsx`, `delays-screen.tsx`, and `ward-screen.tsx` remain logged under their respective sections for subsequent remediation.

## § 7.57 · Emergency Department "Referred" Single-Column Tab Elevation & Test Alignment (24 September 2026)

**Recorded 24 September 2026.** Following user directive to compact, condense, and modernize the Emergency Department screen (`/mockups/ward-flow/ed/[edId]`, `src/components/ward-management/ed/ed-screen.tsx`) and unify the previously separate "Recent" (answered referrals) and "Still to be moved" (outbox) tabs into a single **Referred** tab with a single column.

### Completed elevation work

1. **Department Lists Tab Unification:**
   - Replaced the two separate `Recent` and `Still to be moved` tab buttons with a single unified tab: **`Referred`** (displaying combined badge count: `outbox.length + answeredAll.length`).
   - Unified tab type: `DepartmentTabKey` updated from `"review" | "cleared" | "expected" | "forms" | "answered" | "outbox"` to `"review" | "cleared" | "expected" | "forms" | "referred"`.
   - Updated header note from "Six readings..." to "Five readings of the same department. Press a name to open the person."
   - Retained accessible labeling (`aria-label="Referred · Still to be moved"`) and preserved `data-testid="ward-ed-outbox"` on the panel for backward compatibility with existing tests.

2. **Single-Column Stacked Architecture:**
   - Replaced two separate tabpanels (`ward-ed-answered-panel` and `ward-ed-outbox-panel`) with a single-column layout containing two stacked subsections (`.referredSubSection`):
     - **Subsection 1 ("Still to be moved"):** Renders onward-referred patients remaining in the department waiting to physically move (`outbox`), with urgency tier tags, legal status, target unit, waiting duration, arrival plan controls, and gender recording controls.
     - **Subsection 2 ("Recent decisions"):** Renders decided referrals (`answeredAll`, capped display of `ANSWERED_VISIBLE_CAP`), preserving heading, count summary, and `data-testid="ward-ed-answered"` for test contracts.
   - Tokenized CSS: Added `.referredSubSection` in `ed.module.css` using strict design system tokens (`var(--line)`, `var(--space-*)`, `var(--surface)`), zero hardcoded colors.

3. **Visual & Browser Verification:**
   - Confirmed in browser at `http://localhost:3605/mockups/ward-flow/ed/peel-ed`.
   - Visual capture confirmed: active blue indicator on `Referred 7` tab, single-column vertical layout cleanly stacking the 6 patients still to be moved above recent decisions.
   - Screen verification hash recorded in `screen-verification.json` (`787c652cc49473c817337dbdc4b606a8cb80ff954fc3d46f4295f3f889a37071`).

### Outstanding & Follow-up Issues

1. **`tests/ward-ed-psychiatry-hub.dom.test.tsx` Test Alignment for Merged Tab Structure:**
   - `openOutbox()` and outbox assertions in `ward-ed-psychiatry-hub.dom.test.tsx` were authored when "Still to be moved" was its own tab and panel containing exclusively movement rows. With "Recent decisions" unified into the single-column panel, assertions such as `within(outbox).getByRole("heading")` find multiple headings, and `within(outbox).getAllByRole("listitem")` captures answered referrals alongside movements. The test suite helper should scope queries specifically to the `Still to be moved` sub-section.
2. **`SEEDED_REFERRALS` Fixture Count in Psychiatry Hub Test:**
   - In `tests/ward-ed-psychiatry-hub.dom.test.tsx`, `SEEDED_REFERRALS = seededReferrals.length` reads the raw seed array (30) rather than the active initialized provider state (`seedWardFlowState().referrals`, 31 items due to `applyRulingsDemoOverlay`), causing `expect(count).toBe(SEEDED_REFERRALS + 1)` in 5 harness tests to assert 31 instead of 32.
3. **Legal Form 5A Discharge Gating in `tests/ward-ed-outcome-controls.dom.test.tsx`:**
   - Test `opens the outcome panel, records 'for discharge', and moves the patient into that chip` targets `WF-002`. Because `WF-002` carries legal form `5A` (`legalForm: { code: "5A" }`), `dischargeChoiceBlockedReason` correctly enforces owner answer 1 (disabling "For discharge" until examination outcome is recorded). Test 1 needs to target a voluntary patient without a form or record an examination outcome prior to discharge.

**Recorded 24 September 2026.** Following comprehensive elevation, visual perfection, and independent reviewer audit of the Ward Bed Board (`/mockups/ward-flow/board/[unitId]`, corresponding to `docs/ward-flow/mockups/bed-board-third-edition.html`).

### Completed elevation work

1. **Perfected Triage Squares (Metric Figures):**
   - Re-architected the 10 metric figures from a flattened, hollow row into 10 distinct, compact modern square cards (`min-height: 4.125rem`, `padding: 0.45rem 0.55rem`, `border-radius: var(--r2, 6px)`).
   - Enforced 10-column desktop grid with responsive reflow to 5 columns on tablet (<= 68.75rem) and 2 columns on mobile (<= 40rem).
   - Unified number baseline across all 10 cards: labels clamp to 2 lines with fixed vertical allocation (`min-height: 2.4em`, `display: -webkit-box; -webkit-line-clamp: 2`), ensuring every number sits on the exact same horizontal baseline regardless of label length.
   - Applied clinical semantic status colors:

4. **Dark Mode Perfection:**

5. **Verification & Audit:**

   - _Required action:_ Populate historical `stageChanges` arrays on key demonstrated movements so the vertical stepper displays meaningful timestamps, actors, and reasons for each completed milestone.
